# Go Backend Parity Gap Spec

- Created: 2026-05-14
- Owner: main narrator
- Purpose: track the remaining gaps between `go_backend` and the original TypeScript backend until Go can be considered a safe replacement or a clearly scoped compatibility backend.
- Source of truth rule: this spec must be updated by agents as they claim work, verify evidence, and refine completion status.

## Coordination Protocol

1. Each subagent must claim exactly one track by changing its row from `Unclaimed` to `Claimed: <agent alias>`.
2. Each subagent may only edit its assigned track section plus the coordination log.
3. If a finding affects another track, add a short note to `## Cross-track Coordination Log` and, when useful, send a `TeamStatus` message to the relevant sibling agent.
4. Use statuses consistently:
   - `Unclaimed` — no agent has started this track.
   - `Claimed` — agent is investigating and updating the section.
   - `Drafted` — initial gap list is written but not reviewed against code evidence.
   - `Verified` — evidence paths/line references are checked.
   - `Blocked` — investigation needs a decision or unavailable context.
   - `Done` — spec section has prioritized gaps, notes, and acceptance criteria; it does **not** mean the documented gaps are fixed or that Go is replacement-ready.
5. Do not modify `drizzle/`, do not connect to or mutate `~/.narrafork/narrafork.db`, and do not run migrations.
6. Prefer concrete repo evidence over inference. Mark inference explicitly.

Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentAccountsActiveGoalUsageAndBroadcastsGoalsSet` verifies token/time counters and frontend-visible broadcast updates; verified with the focused Go test, `go test ./internal/narrators -count=1`, and `git diff --check` on touched files.

The focused usage test now covers active-goal counters, `api_requests`, and assistant-message usage metadata; `TestRunAgentLoopMapsProviderLifecycleEvents` covers the provider lifecycle event surface.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: aligned Go narrator `/usage-stats` with the TS `UsageHistoryStats` shape consumed by the details panel. - 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed global usage-history frontend contract parity. - 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed narrator API request raw-dump parity. - 2026-05-27 — main narrator: fixed a chapter delete schema-compatibility gap exposed by all-internal tests. Go chapter deletion now archives/detaches chapter narrators using only available narrator columns, so compact/older test schemas without `narrators.status` still clean `recent_tabs` and delete the chapter instead of failing before tab cleanup.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: repaired Go DB/TS fixture verification after the TS repository switched to a single baseline Drizzle migration while bun-final compatibility artifacts still preserve the 41-entry historical journal. Go migration compatibility now accepts both the bun-final historical journal and the current repository baseline journal after canonical schema readiness, and the TS provider fixture test now runs Bun from the repository root with an isolated HOME so `./drizzle` resolves safely.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed ask-in-passing permission safety parity. Go now rejects `/api/narrators/:id/permission-mode` changes for ask-in-passing narrators until promotion, matching TS's read-only lock instead of allowing accidental escalation to `bypassPermissions`.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed malformed tool-input safety parity. After permission/danger checks but before execution, Go now rejects malformed/truncated tool arguments (including `_raw`) and empty `Write`/`Edit` inputs with failed tool results, preventing file mutation even under `bypassPermissions`/`trust-all`. Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentRejectsMalformedAndEmptyWriteInputsBeforeMutation` verifies no file is created and tool-call rows fail.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed plan-mode permission redirection parity. Non-relaxed Go plan mode now redirects `Write`/`Edit` calls aimed at Markdown files to the designated `.narrafork/plan-{id}.md`, allows that plan-file write under read-only plan mode, appends a notice to the tool result, and persists the redirected `input_json` so stored tool-call audit matches the actual path. Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentPlanModeRedirectsMarkdownWriteToPlanFile` verifies original Markdown path is untouched and the plan file receives the content.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed pending-permission reprocessing/broadcast parity. When Go permission mode switches to `bypassPermissions`, non-question pending tool calls for that narrator are re-resolved through the normal Go permission resolver instead of remaining stuck in `pending`; allow/deny decisions update DB metadata and broadcast `permission_resolved`. Reprocessing now also includes child subagent pending rows when the parent narrator is the broadcast target, while still resolving each row through its owning narrator's permission policy. Subagent pending permission requests now broadcast to the parent narrator target, REST resolution targets the parent broadcast channel, `GET /narrators/:id/permissions` includes child pending permissions with `subagentNarratorId`, and parent-scoped tool-call detail / permission-file-preview routes can read child tool calls using the owning subagent cwd, matching the TS `broadcastTargetId` model; resolved events include `subagentNarratorId` when owner and broadcast target differ. Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentBypassPermissionModeReprocessesPendingTool`, `TestSubagentPendingPermissionBroadcastsToParentNarrator`, `TestParentPermissionsListIncludesSubagentPendingPermission`, `TestParentCanReadSubagentToolCallDetailAndPermissionPreview`, and `TestParentBypassReprocessIncludesSubagentPendingPermission` verifies waiting writes resume after bypass-mode reprocess and subagent permission prompts/list/detail/preview/reprocess events resolve through the parent channel.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed plan-mode soft-deny parity. Non-relaxed Go plan mode now routes the first non-plan-file `Write`/`Edit` implementation attempt through a pending permission with a plan-file hint; when the user approves, Go enables `relaxed_plan`, broadcasts `relaxed_plan_changed`, and executes the original write. Repeated unapproved attempts are denied, and exiting/re-entering plan mode resets the one-shot prompt state. Focused coverage `TestPlanModeStoresPlanStateAndRestoresPermission`, `TestPlanModeRestrictsWriteEditToPlanFile`, and `TestRunBasicAgentPlanModeApprovalEnablesRelaxedPlanAndExecutesWrite` locks the unit and runtime paths.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed ExitPlanMode approval parity. Go no longer lets `ExitPlanMode` directly leave plan mode after readiness reflection; it now prepares the plan/reflection metadata, creates a pending user permission, keeps `plan_mode=1` until approval, and only exits after the permission is approved. Manual plan-mode exit now cancels pending `ExitPlanMode` approvals so the agent does not wait for the full permission timeout. Approval input updates now refresh the tool arguments before execution, so an `updatedPlan` merged into `input_json` is the plan used after approval; Go also stores a TS-style line diff in `input_json.planDiff` and returns it from the tool payload for later continuation prompts. Deny/cancel routes no longer merge `updatedPlan` or `compactAfter`, matching TS where updated input is persisted only on allow, and `permission_resolved` now omits `updatedInput` when no update exists. Plan compact persistence now honors `compactAfter`: Go compacts the approved plan only when requested and otherwise skips `context_summary` mutation. The success payload now includes TS-style `continueMode` and `continuePrompt` metadata for normal, edited-plan, and compact continuations, and approved `ExitPlanMode` now aborts the current run after the tool result, persists the explicit continuation user prompt, and restarts the agent loop to begin execution. `ExitPlanMode` and `AskUserQuestion` are excluded from generic danger reflection to avoid routing plan/user approval tools through the wrong reflection loop. Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentExitPlanReflectionWorksThroughEventLoop`, `TestManualPlanModeExitCancelsPendingExitPlanModeApproval`, `TestCompleteExitPlanModeSkipsPlanCompactWithoutCompactAfter`, `TestExitPlanContinuePromptIncludesDiffAndCompactMode`, `TestMergePermissionInputUpdatesStoresExitPlanDiff`, `TestPermissionApproveRouteMergesExitPlanUpdatedPlanAndCompactAfter`, `TestPermissionDenyRouteDoesNotMergeExitPlanUpdatedPlan`, and `TestPermissionDenyBroadcastsResolvedAndPreservesPendingSideEffectState` verifies plan mode remains active until approval, edited plans are applied through helper/route allow paths only, pending approvals are cancelled on manual exit, no compact is written without `compactAfter`, continuation metadata is emitted and consumed through abort/restart, edited-plan diff metadata is persisted, and deny broadcasts avoid null updated input fields.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed Bash file side-effect parity. Go built-in Bash execution now mirrors TS's before/after `git status --porcelain` snapshot hook: it captures newly dirty paths after Bash, stores first snapshots in `narrator_file_snapshots` with committed `HEAD` content for tracked files and null originals for newly created files, and stores bounded `output_json.bashSnapshots` metadata so rollback/rebuild can replay Bash-created/modified file contents alongside Write/Edit chains without polluting user-visible/model-visible Bash output. Focused coverage `TestBashToolSnapshotsGitStatusDiffFiles` verifies tracked and untracked Bash mutations snapshot and rebuild correctly.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed post-tool git-status side-effect parity. After successful Go `Write`/`Edit`/`Bash` execution for chapter-bound narrators, Go now broadcasts a TS-shaped `git_status` payload with `chapterId`, `toolUseId`, dirty counts, ahead count, base branch, and line totals so frontend git panels can refresh after file-mutating tools. Focused coverage `TestBroadcastGitStatusAfterFileMutatingTool` verifies the broadcast contract.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed narrator hook runtime parity. Go narrator execution now reads enabled global/project `hooks` rows, filters by matcher/tool name, sends TS-shaped `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` hook input payloads, executes command hooks with stdin JSON and exit-code-2 blocking semantics, executes HTTP hooks with JSON POST and `{decision:"block"}` parsing, blocks only explicit `PreToolUse` decisions, and treats `PostToolUse` as non-blocking/fail-open after tool completion. Focused coverage `TestRunBasicAgentPreToolUseCommandHookBlocksTool`, `TestPreToolUseHTTPHookBlockDecision`, `TestRunBasicAgentPostToolUseCommandHookReceivesPayloadAndDoesNotBlock`, and `TestPostToolUseHTTPHookReceivesErrorPayload` verifies command/http blocking and post-tool non-blocking payload behavior.
- 2026-05-29 — main narrator: revalidated PostToolUse hook parity against TS `server/services/hook-service.ts`, `server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts`, and Go `go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go`. Current Go already triggers non-blocking/fail-open PostToolUse hooks after handled contextual/external/built-in tool completion, includes TS-shaped `tool_output`/`tool_is_error`/`tool_input` payloads, and broadcasts `hook:executed` telemetry. Rechecked with `go test ./internal/narrators -run 'TestAgentHookExecutionBroadcastsTSCompatibleTelemetry|TestRunBasicAgentPreToolUseCommandHookBlocksTool|TestPreToolUseHTTPHookBlockDecision|TestAgentHTTPHookHonorsHeadersAndTimeoutTelemetry|TestRunBasicAgentPostToolUseCommandHookReceivesPayloadAndDoesNotBlock|TestPostToolUseHTTPHookReceivesErrorPayload' -count=1`.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed sidecar read/hydration parity. Go message loading now hydrates `narrator_sidecars` onto message-level `sideCars`, tool-call `sideCars`, and matching `contentJson` `tool_use.sideCars`, including tool-use-only historical sidecars, so frontend message bubbles and tool inspectors can render TS-compatible sidecar notices. Tool-call detail now also returns `sideCars` when present. Focused coverage `TestMessagesHydrateNarratorSideCars` verifies message/tool/content block hydration.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed update-download preflight contract parity. Go `/api/update/download` now matches TS by returning JSON before opening SSE when trusted update metadata is unavailable (`404 {error:"No update available"}`) or the requested version is stale (`409` with requested/latest versions), while preserving SSE for actual download attempts. Focused coverage `TestUpdateDownloadWithoutTrustedSourceReturnsTSStyleNoUpdateJSON`, `TestUpdateDownloadTrustedNoUpdateReturnsTSStyleNoUpdateJSON`, `TestUpdateDownloadRejectsRequestedVersionMismatch`, and `TestUpdateDownloadRefetchesTrustedMetadataAndIgnoresClientURLs` verifies no misleading SSE success is advertised for preflight failures.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed `/api/settings` optional provider-array response parity. Go now returns TS-style response-only empty `nkpProviders`, `nugProviders`, and `clineProviders` arrays when those optional arrays are absent from persisted settings, while keeping Go config defaults unpersisted. Focused coverage `TestGetSettingsIncludesTSOptionalProviderArraysWithoutPersistingDefaults` plus `go test ./internal/settings -count=1` verifies the response shape and no default persistence.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed dev-build update check/download parity. Go now treats non-release current versions such as raw `go build`'s `go-backend-dev` as non-downloadable while preserving server `latestVersion`, so `/api/update/check` does not expose `releaseInfo`/download sizes and `/api/update/download` returns TS-style JSON 404 instead of opening SSE for a dev/contract build. Release-like semver builds still download from the trusted configured source. Focused coverage `TestUpdateCheckDevBuildDoesNotAdvertiseDownloadableUpdate`; verified with `go test ./internal/systemroutes -count=1`, app update focused tests, and the TS-vs-Go contract harness.
- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed `/api/settings` fresh fixture shape parity. The contract diff harness now canonicalizes object-key order before shape comparison, so `json.settings.shape differs` no longer appears; focused coverage `TestGetSettingsMatchesTSKiroAndNugQuotaResponseShape` plus `go test ./internal/settings -count=1` verifies the behavior.

- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed storage scan SSE parity and main-thread safety. The TS-vs-Go contract harness now streams SSE bodies until terminal `complete`/`error` events instead of waiting for a potentially long-lived response close, and samples `GET /api/storage/scan`. That exposed a Go single-connection SQLite deadlock where `databaseTableSummaries` held `sqlite_master` rows open while issuing per-table `COUNT(*)` queries; Go now collects table names, closes the rows, then counts tables, restoring the TS-compatible `progress/category/.../complete` event sequence. Focused coverage `TestStorageCachedReturnsLastCompletedScan` now uses `SetMaxOpenConns(1)` to lock the deadlock regression, and the full harness reports `diffs: []`.
- 2026-05-29 — main narrator: narrowed Search result item contract proof. The TS-Go contract harness now asserts concrete fixture result rows, not only result types: CJK chapter/message/narrator hits must expose expected ids plus frontend-consumed `snippet`, `matchField`, `matchScore`, status/role/messageRole/narrator/chapter linkage fields, and short `Al` chapter LIKE fallback must keep `matchField:"title"`. Revalidated with `bunx @biomejs/biome check go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts` and full `bun go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts` (`diffs: []`); remaining Search risk is larger realistic relevance ordering rather than baseline item shape.
- 2026-05-29 — main narrator: narrowed Update UI admin-boundary, degraded-error, and trusted-check proof. The TS-Go contract harness now registers an admin and a regular user, asserting regular users can still read `/api/update/check` and `/version` (200) while `/directory`, `/status`, `/cleanup`, `/apply`, and SSE `/download` return 403 on both TS and Go. It also runs a local trusted update server fixture to sample positive `/api/update/check` for admin and regular users, locking `updateAvailable:true`, `latestVersion`, `releaseInfo.version/path`, and `downloadSize/totalSize` response shape, plus a trusted download-error fixture that locks SSE `progress → error` terminal shape without exercising the TS dev-mode zstd success path. A transient trusted-update network error exposed that Go omitted `latestVersion` from `/api/update/check` error payloads while TS always exposes a current/latest version string; Go now initializes `latestVersion` to `currentVersion` before remote lookup, so failed checks stay frontend-shaped and non-downloadable. The download-error fixture also exposed a Go-only extra `checking` progress event; Go now starts update download streams with TS-compatible `downloading` progress before the terminal event. Revalidated with focused `TestUpdateCheckErrorStillIncludesLatestVersion`, `bunx @biomejs/biome check go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts`, and full `bun go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts` (`diffs: []`).

Revalidated with `bunx @biomejs/biome check go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts` and full `bun go_backend/testtools/ts-go-api-contract-diff.ts` (`diffs: []`); no Go route change was needed because Go already wraps these with `adminOnly`/storage admin guards.

The new fixture exposed a Go `PATCH /api/settings` response drift where optional empty provider arrays such as `nkpProviders` were present on TS but omitted on Go; Go PATCH responses now reuse the GET response normalization (`ensureOptionalProviderArraysForResponse` and provider compatibility normalization) before sanitizing secrets. - 2026-05-29 — main narrator: narrowed frontend split capability consumption safety. The TypeScript backend health response has no `capabilities` object and the TS chapter router does not mount `POST /api/chapters/:id/split`, while the Go backend exposes split through explicit `capabilities.chapters.split.routes.splitAtCommit=true`. `getChapterSplitCapability(undefined)` now defaults to unsupported instead of optimistic supported, so TS/older backends do not show split buttons that would produce 404s; Go remains enabled only when health explicitly advertises split route support, and missing `routes.splitAtCommit` metadata is treated as unsupported rather than optimistic. Focused frontend hook coverage locks the default-disabled, missing-route-disabled, and Go-supported/compressed-fallback cases.
- 2026-05-29 — main narrator: aligned Go split capability metadata with the implemented route semantics: health/graph domain capabilities now report `chapters.split.mode="split-at-commit"` instead of the earlier stale basic-split label, while compressed AI summaries remain explicitly fallback-gated.
- 2026-05-30 — main narrator: locked split health metadata at both route-test and TS-Go semantic-contract levels. `TestPhase0HealthCapabilitiesExposeKnownGoFallbacks` now asserts `capabilities.chapters.split.routes.splitAtCommit=true`, and the TS-Go contract harness now asserts Go health advertises `routes.splitAtCommit=true`, `mode="split-at-commit"`, and the frontend-consumed compressed AI summary fields (`supported=false`, `fallback=true`, deterministic placeholder mode, and reason) before frontend split gating can enable the action. Frontend split capability consumption now also treats missing `partials.compressedAISummary` metadata as not supported instead of optimistic support, so older/partial Go capability payloads still show the compressed-summary fallback warning. Focused split fixture coverage now also asserts the route JSON describes the historical prefix, continuation, and new fork commit boundaries, and compressed split inherit records an explicit unsupported `compressedContext` fallback, bounded copied prefix refs, persisted fallback metadata, and a deterministic fallback summary instead of silently pretending an AI summary was generated. Frontend update capability consumption now treats `update.manualOnly=true` as overriding missing or optimistic self-update/apply fields, preventing partial Go/manual-only payloads from re-enabling auto-apply UI.
- 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed MCP transport capability fallback safety. `getMcpTransportsCapability(undefined)` still preserves TS/legacy behavior by defaulting all transports to supported when no capability object exists, but once an `mcp` capability object is present, non-stdio transports now require explicit `supported:true`; missing Go `mcp.transports.sse` or `streamableHttp` metadata no longer enables unsupported persistent/non-stdio runtimes. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed benchmark container-execution capability fallback safety. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed runtime maintenance capability fallback safety. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed storage cleanup capability fallback safety. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed filesystem/settings system-action capability fallback safety. Legacy TS/no-capability payloads still default filesystem browse/shortcuts/mkdir/preview/reveal and settings TLS generation to supported, but once a capability payload exists, each FS action requires explicit support and TLS generation requires `settings.tlsGeneration=true`; partial Go payloads missing FS/TLS metadata no longer expose reveal/path actions or the TLS generation button.

- 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed narrator runtime/tool capability fallback safety. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed native provider-block replay. - 2026-05-30 — main narrator: locked subagent history replay parity with a DB fixture. Current Go behavior matches the TS provider-history contract for `parentToolUseId`: primary narrators filter child-tree messages out of model history, while subagent narrators treat their own `parent_tool_use_id`-tagged user/assistant/tool-result transcript as top-level provider history. Focused and full `internal/narrators` tests cover the parent filter and subagent replay path.
- 2026-05-30 — main narrator: narrowed PreToolUse lifecycle ordering parity. Go now matches TS `tool-executor.ts` by running `PreToolUse` hooks after permission resolution but before malformed/`_raw` and empty `Write`/`Edit` guard handling, and hook payload construction preserves malformed raw input as `tool_input._raw` instead of silently passing `{}`. Focused coverage verifies a blocking `PreToolUse` hook sees `_raw` and wins before the malformed-input guard; full `internal/narrators` remains green.
- 2026-05-30 — main narrator: closed several special-tool and path fallback permission bypasses. Go now mirrors TS `resolvePermissionDecision`/`shouldAutoAllowSendWithinScope` for `Agent`, `Send`, `TeamStatus`, `AddGoal`, and `Terminal`, and no longer executes these tools before permission resolution where TS requires approval. `readOnly` narrators may only launch `explore`/`plan` Agent subagents, read-only Agent workdirs outside cwd ask for approval, write-capable Agent workdirs ask/deny according to permission mode, directory blacklists deny Agent workdirs before `bypassPermissions`, `Send` auto-allows only when every resolved target's permission rank is less than or equal to the caller's rank, and mode-only special tools such as `TeamStatus`/`AddGoal`/`Terminal` ask in `default`/`acceptEdits` while denying in `readOnly`/`dontAsk`. Go's generic fallback is now path-aware like TS: `Read`/`Glob`/`Grep`/`ShareFile` auto-allow only for in-cwd paths (external paths ask/deny by mode unless whitelisted), `.git` write targets are hard-denied for `Write`/`Edit`/Browser screenshots and common Bash destructive/redirection writes even in `bypassPermissions`, and Bash destructive operations on the project root, project-root ancestors, and `.worktrees` are hard-denied when the narrator is chapter-bound and `project.git_path` is available. Bash command whitelist decisions are now mode-sensitive like TS: `default` asks, `dontAsk` denies, `acceptEdits`/`bypassPermissions` allow, and `readOnly` only auto-allows commands that do not look write-capable. Common Bash command argument paths and redirection targets now participate in directory blacklist/whitelist checks with read-vs-write operation sensitivity and are resolved relative to the Bash `workdir` like TS. A TS-style catastrophic Bash hard-deny subset now blocks recursive deletes of critical paths, `dd` writes to block devices/system paths, recursive permission changes on system directories, and simple fork bombs before `bypassPermissions`. Safe Bash commands now follow TS mode behavior for the covered subset: read-only safe commands can auto-allow in `readOnly`, and safe worktree-local write helpers such as `touch` can auto-allow in `acceptEdits` while dangerous commands such as `rm` still ask. `WebFetch` now follows TS mode behavior after URL policy checks, including `bypassPermissions` auto-allow and `dontAsk` deny. `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` now suppress premature running tool-call records in every mode and resolve as always-ask like TS. `WebSearch` remains always-allow, and unknown tools such as `Browser` ask in default/acceptEdits and deny in readOnly. Tool-call event bookkeeping also treats non-always-allow tools as permission-gated in default/acceptEdits so asks do not first create stale running records. Focused unit/runtime coverage proves readOnly general `Agent`, readOnly-to-higher-rank `Send`, readOnly `TeamStatus`, external `Read`, and readOnly `Browser` are denied/asked before side effects, and full `internal/narrators` tests remain green.

- 2026-05-27 — main narrator: narrowed sidecar model-context parity. Go agent history now formats hydrated sidecars with TS-compatible `<side_car source="...">` blocks, appends `tool_result` sidecars to model-visible tool outputs, and emits `user_message` sidecars as follow-up user context after assistant turns. Go agent events now also accept `SideCars`, include them in `tool_completed`/`sidecars` broadcasts, and persist `tool_result` / standalone `user_message` sidecars into `narrator_sidecars` when the table exists. Go runtime also has sidecar collector paths for provider-boundary `tool_result` and `after_tools` `user_message` injection; TODO reminder sidecars enter the same active loop's fourth completed non-`TaskCreate` tool result, silent-progress sidecars inject after a configurable run of tool calls without visible text, relaxed-plan sidecars remind on non-read-only tools while `plan_mode && relaxed_plan` with a lightweight Go shell write classifier covering common TS `analyzeShellCommand(...).hasWriteOperation` cases (redirection while ignoring `[[ ... > ... ]]` / `(( ... > ... ))` comparison operators, mutating path commands, sed/find/xargs/git/package-runner/package-manager writes), priority buffered messages including command text, image blocks, and text-file attachment hints can be consumed as `buffered_user` after-tools sidecars in the same active loop while non-priority buffers wait for run completion, and the busy-narrator REST buffer path now preserves raw slash-command `command_text` plus persists multipart text attachments under `~/.narrafork/buffered-files` before materializing them back into the worktree without duplicating `<attached_files>` hints when consumed. Go hook execution now emits a TS-compatible `hook:executed` telemetry broadcast with `hookId`, `event`, `hookType`, `outcome`, `narratorId`, and `durationMs` for both blocking `PreToolUse` and async `PostToolUse` hooks, and focused tests lock HTTP hook custom headers plus timeout fail-open telemetry. Active target TeamStatus send/broadcast messages can be delivered through an in-memory inbox and drained as `team_message` after-tools sidecars, completed background agent tasks are queued and drained as `bg_agent` after-tools sidecars, and `Bash(run_in_background=true)` now creates awaitable `background_tasks` rows, broadcasts live `background_task_output` byte counts while stdout/stderr grow, drains completion notices as `bg_bash` after-tools sidecars, and the cancel route cancels the registered background Bash process tree instead of only flipping DB status: Unix commands run in their own process group and Windows falls back to `taskkill /T /F` plus process kill. External goal-list changes while a narrator run is active are drained as one-shot `goal_update` after-tools sidecars, and after-tools sidecars are emitted as `sidecars` events plus next-turn user context for adapters that support user text with tool results. Go event persistence now also resets assistant draft state on `turn_complete`, so a tool-use assistant message and the final answer assistant message are stored as separate rows across multi-turn tool loops. Focused Go tests cover the out-of-range and in-range boundary cases, and the TS-Go contract harness adds `ts-go-storage-database-validation-bounds-smoke` proving both backends reject the same out-of-range values with 400 and accept the in-range boundary. - 2026-06-11 — Wave 282 (Terminal tab/view-state Zod validation parity) closes three terminal HTTP validation-gap families. Go now mirrors the TypeScript Zod schemas: `POST /api/terminals/tabs` requires exactly one of chapterId or narratorId (XOR, 400 on missing/ambiguous), `PUT /api/terminals/view-state` requires at least one of chapterId/narratorId (400 when absent), and the view-state `layout` field is validated against the `single|split-h|split-v|triple|quad` enum (400 on invalid). Focused Go tests cover the missing/ambiguous/valid target and invalid/valid layout cases. Health flips `terminal.httpValidationGapContract.tabCreateRequiresExactlyOneTargetInGo:true`, `viewStateTargetRequiredInGo:true`, `viewStateLayoutEnumValidation:true`, moves those families from `unclaimedValidationFamilies` to `sampledValidationFamilies`, and flips `terminal.tabContract.createRequiresExactlyOneTarget:true`, `terminal.viewStateContract.targetRequiredOnPut:true` / `layoutEnumValidation:true`. `terminal.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` stays because process-snapshot empty-error fallback parity, panel-assignments record validation, and broader terminal panel frontend smoke remain unproven.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 283 (Terminal view-state panel-assignments record validation) closes the remaining terminal view-state validation family. Go now mirrors the TypeScript `panelAssignments: z.record(z.string(), z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.string())])).nullable().optional()` schema: each value must be a string or array of strings, otherwise `PUT /api/terminals/view-state` returns 400; null/omitted assignments stay valid. Focused Go tests cover invalid scalar value, invalid array item, valid string+array, and null cases. Health flips `terminal.httpValidationGapContract.viewStatePanelAssignmentsValidation:true` and `terminal.viewStateContract.panelAssignmentsValidation:true`, and moves `view-state-panel-assignments` from `unclaimedValidationFamilies` to `sampledValidationFamilies`. `terminal.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` stays because process-snapshot empty-error fallback parity and broader terminal panel frontend smoke remain unproven.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 284 (Terminal process-snapshot empty-error fallback parity) aligns the `GET /api/terminals/{id}/processes` error behavior with TypeScript. The TS `getProcessInfoBatch` swallows `ps`/snapshot failures (`catch { /* fallback: empty */ }`) and returns an empty process list, never a 500. Go's processes handler now mirrors that: a `ProcessTree` failure logs non-fatally via slog and returns an empty 200 array instead of surfacing the error. A focused Go test forces a `ps` snapshot failure and asserts the handler returns `[]` with status 200 (while the low-level `ProcessTree` function still surfaces the error to callers). Health flips `terminal.processTreeContract.surfaceSnapshotErrors:false` / `tsErrorFallbackParity:true`, adds `emptyOnSnapshotError:true`, flips `terminal.httpValidationGapContract.processSnapshotErrorFallbackParity:true`, and moves `process-snapshot-empty-error-fallback` into `sampledValidationFamilies`. `terminal.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` stays because batch process snapshot parity and broader terminal panel frontend smoke remain unproven.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 285 (Terminal create Zod bound parity) aligns `POST /api/terminals` create-body validation with the TypeScript createTerminalSchema. Go now rejects out-of-range `name` (max length 100), `cols` (int 10..500), and `rows` (int 2..200) with 400 instead of silently clamping; in-range values still create the terminal and omitted values keep the 80×24 defaults. A focused Go test covers name-too-long, cols/rows out-of-range, and in-range cases. Health adds `terminal.crudContract.createNameMaxLength:100`, `createColsBounds:"10..500"`, `createRowsBounds:"2..200"`, `createBoundsValidation:true`, and samples `crud-create-name-cols-rows-bounds`. `terminal.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` stays because batch process snapshot parity and broader terminal panel frontend smoke remain unproven.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 286 (Terminal PATCH name validation parity) aligns `PATCH /api/terminals/{id}` name validation with TypeScript. The TS route throws a 400 `ValidationError` when `name` is present but is not a non-empty trimmed string; Go previously ignored an invalid/empty name silently. Go now returns 400 `name must be a non-empty string` for empty/whitespace/non-string names while still applying valid renames and graph-only updates. A focused Go test covers empty/whitespace/non-string rejection plus valid rename and graph-only updates, and asserts invalid attempts do not mutate the stored name. Health adds `terminal.crudContract.patchNameMustBeNonEmptyString:true` and samples `crud-patch-name-non-empty`. `terminal.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` stays because batch process snapshot parity and broader terminal panel frontend smoke remain unproven.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 287 (Chapter create Zod bound parity) aligns `POST /api/chapters` create-body validation with the TypeScript createChapterSchema. Go now rejects an empty `projectId`, an empty/missing `title`, a `title` longer than 200 characters, and a `description` longer than 2000 characters with 400 (`projectId is required` / `title is required` / `title must be at most 200 characters` / `description must be at most 2000 characters`) before touching git or the database, instead of relying on a downstream 404 or silently accepting overlong values. Health adds `chapters.create.inputValidationParity:true`, `projectIdRequired:true`, `titleBounds:"1..200"`, `descriptionMaxChars:2000`, and samples `create-projectid-title-description-zod-bounds`.
- 2026-06-11 — Wave 288 (Chapter fork Zod bound parity) aligns `POST /api/chapters/{id}/fork` body validation with the TypeScript forkChapterSchema. All reject with 400 before touching git/the database. Focused Go route-contract tests cover merge-message, review locale/title/crossOffset, and batch-fork empty. The TS-Go contract harness adds `chapterMergeOverlongMessage` and `chapterReviewInvalidLocale` proving both backends reject them with 400 (batch-fork is intentionally a Go-only route — TypeScript defines `batchForkSchema` but never mounts it, so health records `chapters.fork.batchForkTSRouteParity:false` / `batchForkGoExtension:true`). Health adds `chapters.merge.inputValidationParity:true` / `strategyEnum` / `messageMaxChars:500`, `chapters.review.inputValidationParity:true` / `localeEnum` / `titleBounds:"1..200"` / `crossOffsetMin:0`, and `chapters.fork.batchForkCountBounds:"1..10"`, with `review-locale-title-crossoffset-zod-bounds` and `batch-fork-count-bounds-go-extension` sampled.

- 2026-06-11 — Wave 296 (Narrator fork / fork-messages / compact-summary Zod bound parity) aligns three more narrator routes with their TypeScript schemas, while honoring two TypeScript route-specific quirks discovered via the harness. `POST /api/narrators/{id}/fork` now requires a non-empty `forkMessageUuid` and rejects a >200-char `title`; `POST /api/narrators/{id}/fork-messages` enforces `messageIds` 1..500 with non-empty entries and `title` max 200; `PATCH /api/narrators/{id}/segment-compact/{messageId}` enforces `summary` trimmed 1..100000. The plain `PATCH /api/narrators/{id}/compact/{messageId}` route deliberately keeps the looser TypeScript check (reject only an empty/non-string `summary`, no trim, no max) because TypeScript only applies the strict `updateSegmentCompactSummarySchema` to the segment-compact route. The fork-messages cross-backend harness assertion is intentionally omitted: TypeScript uses `forkFromMessagesSchema.parse()` (not safeParse), so an empty `messageIds` array throws an uncaught ZodError and returns 500 in TypeScript, whereas Go returns a clean 400 — Go is the more correct behavior here and is not regressed to match the TS 500. Focused Go tests cover fork missing-uuid/overlong-title, fork-messages empty/too-many/overlong-title, plain compact empty-string, and segment-compact empty/overlong. The harness adds `narratorForkMissingUuid` (both 400). Health extends `narrator.fieldUpdates` with `forkMessageUuidRequired:true`, `forkMessagesCountBounds:"1..500"`, `segmentCompactSummaryBounds:"1..100000"`, `compactSummaryRequiresNonEmpty:true`, and samples `fork-forkmessages-compactsummary-zod-bounds`.

- 2026-06-11 — Wave 299 (Git panel commit/reset/stash/stage Zod bound parity) aligns the git mutation routes with their TypeScript schemas: `POST /api/chapters/{chapterId}/git/commit` requires a trimmed `message` 1..500, `POST .../git/reset` requires `target` 1..100 plus a `mode` enum of `soft`/`hard`, `POST .../git/stash` caps `message` at 200 chars, and `POST .../git/stage` (plus unstage/discard) requires either a non-empty `files` array or `all:true`. All reject with a clean 400. Focused Go tests cover commit empty/overlong message, reset empty/overlong target and invalid mode, stash overlong message, and stage neither-files-nor-all. The cross-backend harness assertions are intentionally omitted because every TypeScript git route uses `schema.parse()` (not safeParse): an empty commit message surfaces as 422 and invalid reset mode / empty stage body surface as uncaught ZodError 500 in TypeScript, whereas Go returns a consistent 400 — Go is the more correct behavior and is not regressed to match the inconsistent TS 422/500. Health extends `gitPanel.inputValidation` with `commitMessageBounds:"1..500"`, `resetTargetBounds:"1..100"`, `resetModeEnum:["soft","hard"]`, `stashMessageMaxChars:200`, `stashActionGoExtension:"apply"`, `stageRequiresFilesOrAll:true`, `goReturns400TSParseQuirk` recording the TS 422/500 quirk, and samples `git-commit-reset-stash-stage-zod-bounds`.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 300 (Ruler merge strategy/message Zod bound parity) aligns `POST /api/projects/{id}/ruler/merge` with the TypeScript `rulerMergeSchema`: `strategy` is now restricted to the `merge`/`squash` enum (defaulting to `merge`) and `message` is capped at 500 characters, both rejected with a clean 400. This is narrower than the chapter merge route, which also accepts `cherry-pick`; the ruler route does not. Validation now runs before the chapter-service-availability fallback so input errors surface as 400 ahead of the 501 `FEATURE_DISABLED` fallback, matching TypeScript's safeParse-first ordering. Unlike the git routes, the TypeScript ruler route uses `safeParse()` → `ValidationError` (400), so cross-backend harness assertions are valid here: the shared harness adds `rulerMergeInvalidStrategy` (strategy `cherry-pick`) and `rulerMergeOverlongMessage` (501-char message), both 400 on TypeScript and Go. A focused Go test covers invalid/unknown strategy and overlong message rejection plus default/explicit-merge/squash/at-limit acceptance reaching the feature-disabled fallback. Health extends `ruler.mergeInputValidation` with `mergeStrategyEnum:["merge","squash"]`, `mergeStrategyDefault:"merge"`, `mergeMessageMaxChars:500`, `rebaseResolveActionEnum:["abort","continue"]`, `validatesBeforeServiceFallback:true`, mirrors `mergeInputValidationParity`/`mergeStrategyEnum`/`mergeMessageMaxChars` into `ruler.replacementContract`, and samples `ruler-merge-strategy-message-zod-bounds`.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 301 (Project update chapterSettings nested Zod bound parity) aligns `PATCH /api/projects/{id}` `chapterSettings` validation with the nested bounds of the TypeScript `updateProjectSchema`. Previously Go only stripped unknown chapterSettings keys before merge/persist; it now also rejects out-of-range nested arrays/objects with 400 before persistence, matching the settings-route policy validators. Enforced bounds: `commands` array max 100 (each command: `name` 1..50 + `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$` regex, `prompt` 1..400000, `description` ≤500, `bashCommand` ≤400000, `params` max 20 with `name` 1..50/`description` ≤500/`defaultValue` ≤1000, `modelOverride.model` 1..100 + `mode` enum temporary/permanent); `whitelistDirs`/`blacklistDirs` array max 50 (each: `path` trimmed 1..4096, `accessLevel` enum readOnly/readWrite/full or `denyLevel` enum denyWrite/denyAll, `enabled` bool); `commandWhitelist`/`commandBlacklist` array max 50 (each: `pattern` trimmed 1..200, `denyPrompt` ≤2000 for blacklist, `enabled` bool). Since the TypeScript update route uses `safeParse()` → `ValidationError` (400), cross-backend harness assertions are valid: the shared harness adds `projectUpdateInvalidCommandName` (bad name regex), `projectUpdateOverlongCommandPattern` (201-char pattern), and `projectUpdateInvalidAccessLevel` (invalid enum), all 400 on TypeScript and Go. Focused Go tests cover all nested rejection cases plus a fully-valid chapterSettings acceptance case. Health adds a `projects.update` capability (`route:"PATCH /api/projects/{id}"`, `inputValidationParity:true`, name/description/status/startupScript/copyFiles/proxyDomain bounds) with a nested `chapterSettingsValidation` block recording every bound and enum, and samples `update-name-description-status-zod-bounds` plus `update-chaptersettings-commands-dir-command-rules-zod-bounds`. The flat name/description/status/branch/proxyDomain bounds were already enforced (Wave 291 note); this wave closes the previously-unvalidated nested chapterSettings layer.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 302 (Narrator/terminal WebSocket client-message Zod bound parity) aligns the Go WS hub handlers with the bounds in the TypeScript `narratorWsMessageSchema`/`terminalWsMessageSchema` discriminated unions. TypeScript runs `schema.safeParse()` on every inbound WS frame and drops invalid frames with a generic `{type:"error",message:"Invalid message format"}`, so out-of-range fields never reach a handler; Go previously dispatched to per-handler logic that only did partial checks and could persist out-of-range data. Go now enforces, before any DB mutation: `buffer_message` text ≤100000; `update_buffer` text 1..100000 when present; `update_timeout` timeoutMs int 1000..86400000; terminal `resize` cols 10..500/rows 2..200; terminal `create` name ≤100 + cols 10..500/rows 2..200 when provided (rejecting before persisting a terminal row); terminal `rename` name 1..100. Each violation emits a targeted Go error frame instead of silently accepting the value. Focused Go WS tests cover overlong buffer text, update_timeout lower/upper bound rejection, invalid terminal create cols (no row persisted), resize rows below 2, and rename name over 100. An existing update_timeout test was corrected to use an in-range 60000ms value (the prior 123ms value would also be rejected by the TypeScript schema). These are WS-frame validations not exercised by the HTTP contract harness, so parity is proven via focused tests + health metadata rather than harness samples. Health adds `narrator.wsClientMessageBounds` (`bufferMessageTextMaxChars:100000`, `updateBufferTextBounds:"1..100000"`, `updateTimeoutMsBounds:"1000..86400000"`, `validatesBeforeDBMutation:true`, samples `narrator-ws-buffer-text-update-timeout-zod-bounds`) and a `terminal.ws.clientMessageBounds` block (`resizeColsBounds:"10..500"`, `resizeRowsBounds:"2..200"`, `createNameMaxChars:100`, `renameNameBounds:"1..100"`, samples `terminal-ws-create-resize-rename-zod-bounds`).

Chapter create/fork/merge/review/cherry-pick bounds were already enforced (Waves 287-289); this wave closes the previously-unvalidated update route.

Go already enforced `systemPrompt` ≤10000, `reasoningEffort` enum, and the plan/danger reflection override enums, but was missing the `cwd` bound; it now rejects an empty (when present) or >4096-char `cwd` with 400 before any narrator/session creation, matching the existing `updateNarratorCwdSchema` bound and the safeParse-first ordering of the TypeScript route. The TypeScript route uses `safeParse()` → `ValidationError` (400), so cross-backend harness assertions are valid: the shared harness adds `narratorCreateOverlongCwd`, `narratorCreateOverlongSystemPrompt`, and `narratorCreateInvalidReasoningEffort`, all 400 on TypeScript and Go (each rejects on a standalone create body before any chapter lookup or session spawn). A focused Go test exercises `validateCreateRequest` for empty/overlong cwd, overlong systemPrompt, invalid reasoningEffort, and both reflection override enums plus a fully-valid acceptance case. The narrator update/field-update bounds were already enforced (Waves 287-298); this wave closes the previously-unvalidated `cwd` gap on the create route.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 305 (Git log skip pagination + limit/skip bound parity) closes a functional gap on `GET /api/chapters/{chapterId}/git/log`. Previously Go parsed `limit` with no bounds and ignored the `skip` query param entirely, so the git panel could not paginate commit history; TypeScript's `gitService.getLog` passes `--skip` and `--max-count` from `gitLogQuerySchema` (limit 1..200 default 50, skip ≥0 default 0). Go now adds `gitx.Service.LogPaged(path, limit, skip)` that clamps limit to 1..200 (default 50) and applies a non-negative `--skip` offset, and the route parses both params: missing params use the defaults while explicitly out-of-range values (`limit<1`, `limit>200`, non-numeric `limit`, negative `skip`) reject with 400. A focused Go test (`TestGitLogRouteSupportsSkipPaginationAndValidatesBounds`) seeds four commits and asserts full listing order, `skip=1` dropping the newest commit, `limit` capping the count, and all four out-of-range rejections. The shared harness adds `gitLogSkipBeyondHistory` (`?limit=5&skip=1`): the single-commit fixture pages past its only commit so both backends return an empty array (a valid-value cross-backend assertion that avoids the TS git `.parse()` 422/500 quirk documented in Wave 299). Health extends `gitPanel.inputValidation` with `logLimitBounds:"1..200"`, `logLimitDefault:50`, `logSkipMin:0`, `logSkipPaginationParity:true`, and samples `git-log-limit-skip-pagination-bounds`.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 306 (Dependency install unknown-name status parity) aligns `POST /api/dependencies/{name}/install` with the TypeScript dependencies route. TypeScript validates `name` against the `["git","rg","dtach"]` allow-list and throws a `ValidationError` (400) before attempting any install command; Go previously fell through to `installDependency`, which returned `{"ok":false,"error":"Unknown dependency: ..."}` serialized as a 500. Go now rejects an unknown dependency name with a 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR` (`Invalid dependency name: <name>`) before the install command, matching TypeScript's status and message ordering (the admin-only guard still runs first). A focused Go test (`TestDependencyInstallRejectsUnknownNameWith400`) covers several unknown names with an admin token, and the shared harness adds `dependencyInstallUnknownName` proving both backends return 400. Health adds a top-level `systemDependencies` capability (`statusRoute:"GET /api/dependencies"`, `installRoute:"POST /api/dependencies/{name}/install"`, `installAdminOnly:true`, `installableNames:["git","rg","dtach"]`, `unknownNameRejectsWith:400`, `unknownNameErrorCode:"VALIDATION_ERROR"`, `validatesBeforeInstall:true`) and samples `dependency-install-unknown-name-400`. This is a status-code parity fix (500→400) rather than a new validation surface; the valid install path behavior is unchanged.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 307 (Global prompt write default-candidate parity) closes a functional gap on `PUT /api/routines/global-prompt`. TypeScript, when the request omits `filePath`, writes to the first *existing* candidate (`~/.agents/AGENT.md` then `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`), only falling back to the first candidate when none exist — so an existing `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` is updated in place. Go previously always defaulted to `candidates[0]` (`~/.agents/AGENT.md`), creating a new file and silently ignoring an existing `CLAUDE.md`. Go now scans candidates for the first existing file before falling back to `candidates[0]`, matching the TypeScript write path (the GET read path already picked the first existing file, and the allow-list/`filePath` validation is unchanged). Two focused Go tests cover the existing-CLAUDE.md-updated-in-place case (AGENT.md not created) and the no-candidate fallback to AGENT.md. The write path mutates files under `$HOME` shared between the two backends in sequence, so it is proven via focused tests + health metadata rather than a cross-backend harness sample (the existing read-only `routinesGlobalPrompt` GET sample is unchanged). Health extends `content.routines.globalPromptContract` with `writeDefaultsToFirstExistingCandidate:true` and `writeFallbackCandidate:"~/.agents/AGENT.md"`.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 308 (Drizzle migration baseline resync for `0041_exotic_nekra`) closes a P0 DB-parity regression: the TypeScript schema gained a new Drizzle migration `0041_exotic_nekra` that adds the `skill_directory_caches` table (id/root_kind/normalized_root_path/skills_json/signature_json/scanned_at/last_accessed_at/expires_at plus three indexes), but the Go embedded migration baseline still stopped at `0040_talented_pandemic`. This broke `TestCurrentRepositoryBaselineSnapshotMatchesRepository` (the Go baseline snapshot no longer matched the repo `drizzle/meta/_journal.json`) and `TestGoRoundtripFixtureSurvivesBunReadAndGoReopen` (Go bootstrap did not create the new table, so the schema hash diverged after Bun applied the migration). Regenerated `go_backend/internal/db/drizzle_migrations_embedded.go` via `scripts/generate-go-drizzle-migrations.ts` (now 42 migrations, including the `skill_directory_caches` DDL with SQL hash `5b6892b1717ea94d0d6768c9ee9765200662cfc2dedccc6a8b87b69f11f68d7c`), synced the static `drizzle_journal_snapshot.go` fallback list, and regenerated the canonical schema contract artifact via `go run ./cmd/schema-contract` (migration count 41→42, last migration → `0041_exotic_nekra`, expected migration hash updated; `canonicalSchemaHash` unchanged). The `skill_directory_caches` table is intentionally NOT added to the read-only canonical schema contract map because the Go backend does not yet read/write the TS skill-directory scan cache — Go's bootstrap creates the table for TS write compatibility, but the canonical contract only tracks tables Go depends on. Full Go test suite passes.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 309 (Skill directory scan strategy capability contract) makes the `skill_directory_caches` divergence introduced with migration `0041` an explicit, accepted capability contract instead of an implicit gap. TypeScript caches skill-directory scan results in the `skill_directory_caches` DB table (signature-based invalidation, 7-day max-age, hourly cleanup timer, per-root bounds), while Go performs a live filesystem scan of `.narrafork/skills`, `.narrafork/skill`, `.claude/skills`, and `.agents/skills` (max depth 5) on every list request with no persistent directory cache. The list/detail API output is identical — the cache is a TypeScript-side performance optimization only. Audit confirmed `skill_directory_caches` is the single TS table excluded from Go's canonical schema contract map, and that exclusion is correct because Go neither reads nor writes the cache. A focused Go test (`TestProjectSkillScanIsLiveAndReflectsNewlyAddedSkills`) proves the live-scan behavioral contract: adding or removing a skill is reflected immediately on the next scan with no cache-invalidation step. Health adds a `content.skills.directoryScanContract` block (`goScanStrategy:"live-filesystem-scan"`, `tsScanStrategy:"db-cached-skill-directory-caches"`, `persistentDirectoryCache:false`, `listOutputParity:true`, `scanRoots`, `maxScanDepth:5`, `goDoesNotUseSkillCacheTable:true`, `performanceDifferenceOnly:true`, plus an `acceptedDecision` rationale). This closes the Wave 308 follow-up by documenting the scan-strategy difference as a product-accepted performance divergence rather than leaving it as silent drift.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 310 (Narrator skill-context route implementation) adds the previously-missing `GET /api/narrators/{id}/skills` route to the Go backend. The frontend `NarratorDetailsPanel` (`useNarratorSkills`) consumes this route to display skills available to a narrator's project/cwd context; Go had `/api/skills*` (global/project CRUD) but not the narrator-context resolution route. The new Go handler mirrors TypeScript `resolveSkillContextForNarrator` + `loadSkillSummariesForContext`: it resolves the narrator's project git path and effective cwd (chapter worktree → narrator cwd → project git path → home), builds the global/project/workspace skill roots (deduped by absolute path), scans each root, dedupes skills by name (later roots override), truncates descriptions to 1000 chars, caps files at 20, hides disabled skills, and returns `{skills, roots, scopeKey}`. Per the Wave 309 accepted live-scan contract, each root's cache metadata reports `cacheHit:false`, `refreshed:true`, `cacheable:false` (Go does not use the TS `skill_directory_caches` DB cache). A missing narrator returns 404; the `refresh` query param is accepted for compatibility but has no effect. Two focused Go tests cover context-scoped summaries (global+project sources, disabled hidden, live-scan root meta, scope key) and the missing-narrator 404. The shared harness adds `narratorSkillsContext` (GET against the fixture narrator), asserting both backends return 200 with matching `{skills, roots, scopeKey}` shape and valid rootKinds (global/project/workspace); the per-root cache metadata is additive and not cross-asserted. Health adds a `narrator.skillsRoute` capability (route, response fields, rootKinds, dedupe/disabled-hidden/bounds, `scanStrategy:"live-filesystem-scan"`, `rootCacheMeta`, `missingNarratorStatus:404`) with sample `narrator-skills-context-scoped-summaries`.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 311 (Contract-matrix test-reference integrity) fixes stale test-function references in the phase0/phase4 contract matrices that pointed to renamed/removed Go test functions, weakening traceability. An automated audit of all 122 Go `::TestName` references plus all `.go`/`.ts`/`.tsx` test-file references across the five phase matrices found four dangling Go references and corrected them to the current functions: `chapters/batch_merge_test.go::TestBatchMergeHandlerStartAndSessionRoutes` → `TestBatchMergeRouteReturnsAsyncSessionContractAndSessionEndpoint`; `misccompat/routes_test.go::TestStorageCleanupUnsupportedTargetsReturnStableFallbackWithoutMutation` → `TestStorageCleanupUnsupportedTargetsDoNotFakeSuccessOrMutate`; `systemroutes/handlers_test.go::TestProviderRoutesRequireAuth` → `TestProviderSensitiveRoutesRequireAdminBoundary`; `systemroutes/handlers_test.go::TestCodexUsageQueueClearReturnsOkWhenQueueIsEmpty` → `TestCodexUsageQueueClearReturnsFeatureDisabledWithoutQueueRuntime`. After the fix all 122 Go test references and all referenced test files resolve to existing functions/files (0 dangling), and the four corrected tests pass. No production code changed; this is a documentation/traceability maintenance wave. The eight TS/frontend references initially flagged were verified as false positives (compound `describe`+`test` names and slash-joined harness sample keys that the substring audit could not match), confirming no TS-reference drift remains.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 312 (Image-generation partial-image streaming parity) closes a frontend-consumed streaming gap introduced by the TypeScript `image generation streaming` feature. The frontend (`useNarratorPanelWS.ts`, `MessageBubble.tsx`, `message-segments.ts`) renders live partial-image previews from `image_generation` stream events carrying `partialImageIndex`/`partialImageB64`. TypeScript's OpenAI Responses provider emits these on `response.image_generation_call.partial_image`; Go previously collapsed `partial_image` into the plain `generating` status and dropped the partial preview payload, so Go never streamed partial previews. Go now: adds `PartialImageIndex *int`/`PartialImageB64 string` to `ImageGenerationEvent` and the shared `responsesStreamEvent` parse struct; splits the `partial_image` case from `generating` in both the OpenAI Responses SSE parser and the Codex WebSocket parser (they share `handleResponsesJSONEvent`) via a new `emitResponsesImageGenerationPartial` helper; carries the partial fields through the agent→narrator event bridge (`loop_basic.go` adds them to the `image_generation` event Metadata only when present); and passes them through to the WS frame in `agent_event_handler.go` (only when present, preserving backward compatibility for non-partial events). A focused parser test drives `generating`/two `partial_image`/`completed` events through `handleResponsesJSONEvent` and asserts only the partial events carry the index/base64 (covering both provider paths via the shared parser); the frontend-contract test adds a partial `image_generation` broadcast asserting the WS frame exposes `partialImageIndex`/`partialImageB64` while the completed frame does not. Health adds `narrator.wsEvents.imageGenerationStreaming` (`partialImageStreaming:true`, `partialImageIndexField`/`partialImageB64Field`, `statuses`, `providers:["openai-responses","codex-websocket"]`) with sample `image-generation-partial-image-streaming`. This is a streaming-field augmentation that does not change any HTTP route response, so the contract harness is unaffected.
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 363（Exploration-groups Go-only route 完善：get 返回 branches + list/get/update focused tests）继续 Wave 362 关于 exploration-groups 是 Go-only 路由组的发现。由于该路由组没有 TS 对照（无法用 harness 做跨后端 diff），其正确性完全依赖 Go 自身 focused tests，而此前 6 个操作（list/get/create/update/decide/abandon）只有 create/decide/abandon 有测试，**list/get/update 三个操作完全无 focused 覆盖**。本轮：(1) **修复真实行为不一致**——`getByID`（被 get/update/decide/abandon 响应共用）此前不返回 branches，而 create 响应返回 branches，导致 `getExplorationGroup`（前端 `chapters.ts:205` 调用）拿到的 group 缺 branches；新增 `branchesForGroup` 按 `created_at ASC` 加载 exploration_group_id 关联章节，使 get/update/decide/abandon 响应与 create 一致。(2) 新增 3 个 focused tests：`TestListExplorationGroupsRequiresProjectIdAndReturnsArray`（缺 projectId 400 / 直数组 / 字段集）、`TestGetExplorationGroupReturnsGroupWithBranches`（返回 2 个 branches + 缺失 404）、`TestUpdateExplorationGroupAppliesTitleDescriptionAndValidatesBounds`（应用并持久化 title/description + 空 title 400 + 超长 description 400）。Health 在 `explorations` 新增 `getContract`/`updateContract` 子契约、`goOnlyRouteGroup:true` + reason、3 个新 sampledProofs。所有 exploration 测试通过，app/chapters 无回归，harness `diffs:[]`。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 364（Cherry-pick Go-only route git 执行路径 focused 覆盖）审计发现 `POST /api/chapters/:id/cherry-pick` 是又一处 **Go 超出 TS parity 的 Go-only 路由**（与 Wave 362 的 exploration-groups 同模式）：前端 `frontend/lib/api/chapters.ts:161` 调用该端点，Go 在 `chapters/handlers.go` 完整实现，但 **TypeScript 后端没有任何 cherry-pick 路由**——TS 仅在 `server/lib/validators/chapters.ts:151` 定义了 `cherryPickSchema`，且该 schema **从未被任何 TS 路由 handler 使用**（grep 确认 `server/` 路由层零引用，属 dead schema），同时 `server/routes/chapters.ts` 路由清单无 `/cherry-pick`。因此该端点无 TS 对照、无法用 harness 做跨后端 diff，其正确性完全依赖 Go 自身 focused tests；而此前 git service 的 `CherryPickCommits`（`internal/git/service.go:1074`，实际执行 `git cherry-pick`）与 handler 成功路径**完全无 focused 覆盖**（仅有 route_contract 的请求体校验断言和 ai-resolve 旁路测试）。本轮新增 `internal/git/service_cherrypick_test.go` 3 个真实 git fixture 测试：`TestCherryPickCommitsSuccessAppliesCommitsAndReturnsHead`（在 feature 分支造一个 commit，checkout 回 main 后 cherry-pick，断言 `Success=true`、文件内容已应用、`CommitSHA` 与 repo HEAD 一致、无冲突）、`TestCherryPickCommitsConflictReturnsConflictFilesWithoutError`（main 与 feature 对同一文件冲突修改，断言冲突经 `MergeResult` 报告而非硬错误——`err==nil`、`Success=false`、`ConflictFiles` 含 `shared.txt`）、`TestCherryPickCommitsEmptyListIsNoop`（空 commit 列表是 success no-op）。gofmt 干净，git/chapters 包测试与 `go build ./...` 通过。此处记录该 Go-only 不对称供未来 TS-side 修复或正式接受为 Go-only 增强；本轮只补 Go 实现的测试安全网，不修改路由行为。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 365（Git stash 系列 git 执行路径 focused 覆盖）延续 Wave 364 对"前端消费但 Go service 方法无 focused 测试"的审计：`/api/chapters/:id/git/stash`（push）、`GET .../git/stash/list`、以及 apply/pop/drop 操作由前端 `frontend/lib/api/git.ts` 直接调用，Go 路由组 `internal/gitroutes/handlers.go` 完整实现（与 TS `server/routes/git.ts` 路由级 11 条一一对齐，无缺失），但底层 git service 方法 `Stash`/`StashList`/`StashApply`/`StashPop`/`StashDrop`（`internal/git/service.go:1114-1193`，实际执行 `git stash push/list/apply/pop/drop`）此前**完全无 focused 覆盖**——`internal/git` 测试只覆盖了 discard/diff/log/status/merge-tree/cherry-pick，stash 整组缺测。本轮新增 `internal/git/service_stash_test.go` 3 个真实 git fixture 测试：`TestStashLifecyclePushListApplyDrop`（带 untracked 文件 push → 断言 `--include-untracked` 清空工作区、list 返回 1 条带 index/message、apply 恢复改动且保留 stash 条目、drop 后 list 清空）、`TestStashPopRestoresAndRemovesEntry`（pop 同时恢复改动并移除条目，区别于 apply）、`TestStashPopReportsConflictsWithoutHardError`（stash 后对同文件提交冲突修改，pop 经 `StashResult.HasConflicts` 报告而非硬错误——`err==nil`、`HasConflicts=true`，匹配 handler 把冲突 payload 返回前端的契约）。gofmt 干净，git/gitroutes 包测试与 `go build ./...` 通过。本轮只补 Go 实现的测试安全网，不修改路由行为。

- 2026-06-12 — Wave 387（WebFetch 工具 raw-only 模式门控 fallback 契约 focused 覆盖）审计 Go 后端 `not implemented` 标记后发现 `registerKnownPlaceholders` 已无占位路由、WebFetch 能力契约（`webFetch.mode="raw-only"`）准确、但 **WebFetch 工具运行时对 TS 扩展模式（readability/dom/smart/screenshot）与 selector/purpose 参数的显式门控从未被测**——此前仅 `TestExecutableToolSchema…` 验证 schema 暴露这些模式（为 TS 兼容），无测试验证运行时实际调用这些模式时返回显式 unsupported 消息。注：DOM/Readability 模式的真实实现需要 `golang.org/x/net/html` 依赖，但该依赖不在本地模块缓存且网络不可用（`proxy.golang.org` 超时），且 stdlib 裸标签剥离无法忠实匹配 TS 的 Readability/DOM 提取——故本轮诚实地**只锁定当前 raw-only fallback 契约**而非伪造 parity。新增 `internal/agentbasic/webfetch_mode_gating_test.go` 1 个测试 `TestWebFetchToolGatesUnsupportedModesAndParams`：readability/dom/smart/screenshot 四模式各返回 "unsupported WebFetch mode"+"supported mode: raw" 且不泄露抓取的页面 body → selector/purpose 参数返回 "selector and purpose" 门控消息 → `mode:"RAW"`（大小写不敏感）与缺省 mode 均正常抓取返回 200+body。gofmt 干净，agentbasic 包测试、`go build ./...` 与全套件 `go test ./...` 零失败。本轮只补 Go 实现的测试安全网，不修改工具行为；WebFetch 扩展模式的真实实现仍是需要 `x/net/html` 依赖（当前环境受限）的待办 parity gap。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 388（WebFetch `dom` 模式实现：无浏览器 HTTP-fetch 路径）**真实实现**了之前 Wave 387 标记为待办的 WebFetch dom 模式 parity gap（不再是补测试）。TS 的 WebFetch（`server/lib/agent/tools/web-fetch.ts`，默认 mode=`readability`）文档明确 readability/dom/smart **无需浏览器、Chrome 不可用时回退 HTTP fetch**；Go 此前对所有非 `raw` 模式直接报错——真实行为 parity gap。**依赖解锁**：经核查 `golang.org/x/net@v0.41.0` 在本地模块缓存可离线添加（`go get golang.org/x/net@v0.41.0`，副作用 `x/crypto` v0.45.0→v0.39.0，因 v0.45.0 需未缓存的 net v0.47.0；已验证全套件在该组合下通过，一次 narrators 失败经多次复跑确认为既有并行 flake 非本变更引入）。新增 `internal/agentbasic/webfetch_dom.go`：`fetchDomHTTP` 用 `x/net/html` 实现 TS `fetchDom` 的 HTTP-fetch 回退路径——HTTP 抓取（复用 `webFetchHTTPClient`/UA/代理策略）→ 无 selector 提取 `<body>` 子树 / 有 selector 用简单 CSS selector 子集（tag/`.class`/`#id`/`tag.class`，复合选择器显式拒绝）匹配节点 → `cleanHtml` 丢弃 DISCARD_TAGS（script/style/noscript/svg/iframe/link/meta/head）及其子树、剥离非 KEEP_ATTRS 属性（含 aria-* 前缀）、折叠空白 → maxLength 截断 `[DOM truncated at N characters]`。`openai.go` 的 WebFetch case：`mode=dom`（大小写不敏感）走新实现并支持 selector；readability/smart/screenshot 继续显式 unsupported（消息更新为 "supported modes: raw, dom"）。能力契约 `webFetch.mode` 从 `raw-only` 改为 `raw+dom-http`，新增 `domMode:"http-fetch-no-browser"`/`domRequiresChrome:false`/`readabilityImplemented:false` 等，route_contract_test 同步。新增 `webfetch_dom_test.go` 4 测试（用 httptest 本地 HTML 服务，无需 Chrome）：无 selector 清洗保留可见文本+丢弃 script/style/noscript 内容+保留结构标签、selector 子集（h1/.intro/#lead/aside.sidebar/无匹配/复合拒绝）、maxLength 截断、非 http/https 拒绝；更新 Wave 387 门控测试（dom 移出 unsupported 列表）。gofmt 干净，`go vet`、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。**诚实边界**：本轮只实现 dom 的 HTTP-fetch 路径（无 JS 渲染），CSS selector 仅常见子集，sanitize-html 完整 allow-list 语义用 x/net/html 近似；readability（需 Mozilla Readability 算法）、smart（需 AI provider）、screenshot（需 Chrome）仍是待办 gap，能力契约如实标注 `*Implemented:false`。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 389（WebFetch dom 实现后的工具元数据/schema 描述准确性同步）Wave 388 实现 dom 模式后，发现两处工具自描述元数据仍声称 dom 不被支持（陈旧）：`openai.go` 的 WebFetch schema `mode` 描述说 "Go runtime rejects these modes"、`selector`/`purpose` 描述说 "unsupported in Go raw-only runtime"；`tool_inventory.go` 的 WebFetch 能力元数据 `mode:"raw-only"`、`supportedModes:["raw"]`、`unsupportedModes`/`knownDegraded` 含 `dom`、reason 说 "raw fetches only"。本轮同步为准确状态：schema `mode` 描述改为说明 raw(默认)+dom 支持、selector 描述列出支持的子集语法；tool_inventory `mode:"raw+dom-http"`、`supportedModes:["raw","dom"]`、`unsupportedModes`/`knownDegraded` 移除 dom（仅留 readability/smart/screenshot）、reason 更新。同步更新 `tool_inventory_test.go` 三处 `mode=="raw-only"` 断言为 `"raw+dom-http"`、degraded/unsupported 模式循环移除 dom 并新增"dom 应在 supportedModes 而非 unsupportedModes"的正向断言。gofmt 干净，agentbasic/app 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮只修正工具自描述元数据使其与 Wave 388 实现一致，不改变运行时行为。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 390（WebFetch dom 模式补齐 data: URL 支持，修复 Wave 388 引入的 parity 缺口）审计发现 Wave 388 的 `fetchDomHTTP` 只处理 http/https，而 TS `fetchDom` 经 `httpFetchHtml` **支持 `data:` URL**（`server/lib/web-fetch/http-fetch.ts:45` 显式处理 data: 协议）——这是 Wave 388 引入的真实 parity 缺口（raw 模式支持 data: 但新 dom 模式不支持，不一致）。本轮修复：`fetchDomHTTP` 按 scheme 分派——`data:` 走新增的 `decodeDataURLBytes`（复用 `parseDataURLHeader`，支持 base64 与 percent-encoded payload）解码内联 HTML 后走同样的解析/清洗路径，`http`/`https` 走原 HTTP 抓取，其它 scheme 显式拒绝。新增 2 测试：`TestWebFetchDomModeHandlesDataURL`（`data:text/html,...` 内联 HTML 经 dom 清洗保留可见文本、丢弃 script 内容、无网络抓取）、`TestWebFetchDomModeRejectsUnsupportedScheme`（file: 仍被拒绝，消息更新为 "only http, https, and data"）。能力契约 webFetch `protocols` 已含 data（无需改）。gofmt 干净，agentbasic 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮修复 dom 模式与 TS 在 data: URL 上的行为差异，使 dom 与 raw 模式的协议支持一致。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 391（WebFetch dom 模式代理策略 consumption focused 覆盖）补齐 dom 模式对 WebFetch 代理策略的 focused 测试。dom 模式经 `webFetchRawHTML` 复用 `webFetchHTTPClient(ctx)`，因此与 raw 模式一样消费 `WebFetchProxyPolicy`（能力契约 `webFetch.policy="permission-policy-and-proxy-consumed"` 覆盖所有模式），但此前只有 raw 模式有代理测试（`TestWebFetchToolUsesCustomProxyPolicy`），dom 路径未验证。新增 `TestWebFetchDomModeUsesCustomProxyPolicy`：配置 custom 代理 → dom 模式 HTTP 抓取经代理路由（proxyHit + 代理返回的 body 出现在清洗输出中 + script 仍被丢弃）。gofmt 干净，agentbasic 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮只补 dom 模式代理消费的测试安全网，锁定其与 raw 模式行为一致，不改变运行时行为。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 392（WebSearch 工具后端差异 capability contract 显式化）审计发现 WebSearch 工具存在**未文档化的架构差异**：Go `toolWebSearch` 使用独立的 DuckDuckGo Instant Answer 查询，而 TS（`server/lib/agent/tools/web-search.ts`）经 provider-backed MCP search 链（kiro → nug → nkp fallback）；且 Go WebSearch 此前落入 `tool_inventory` 的 `default` 分支（通用契约，`runtimeCompatible:full`），**未标注该差异**——与 WebFetch/Browser/Read 都有详细 capability contract 不一致。本轮为 WebSearch 新增显式 capability contract：`parity:partial`、`runtimeCompatible:partial`、`searchBackend:"duckduckgo-instant-answer"`、`providerMcpSearch:false`、`fallbackBehavior:"standalone_duckduckgo"`、`knownDegraded:["provider_mcp_search"]`、reason 说明 Go 用 DuckDuckGo 独立查询而 TS 用 provider-MCP 链、结果不同但无需配置 provider 即可用。新增 `websearch_contract_test.go` 1 测试 `TestWebSearchCapabilityContractDocumentsBackendDivergence` 锁定该契约字段。gofmt 干净，agentbasic 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮只把已存在的真实后端差异从"未文档化"升级为"显式 capability contract + 测试锁定"，不改变运行时行为；WebSearch 与 TS 的 provider-MCP parity 仍需 live provider（kiro/nug/nkp），属环境/决策受限的待办。

- 2026-06-12 — Wave 402（self-restart EnvForSession 握手环境契约 focused 覆盖）继续基础设施包审计，发现 `internal/restart` 的导出函数 `EnvForSession`（自重启时为新进程构建握手环境变量）此前**无测试**——现有测试只覆盖 graceful-shutdown token gate 与 HTTP handoff post，未验证环境变量构建。`EnvForSession` 产生的四个 `KEY=VALUE` 必须与新进程经 `StartupHandoffFromEnv` 读回的 env key（EnvURL/EnvToken/EnvMarkerPath/EnvMarkerNonce）一致，否则自重启握手断裂。新增 `internal/restart/env_for_session_test.go` 2 测试：`TestEnvForSessionProducesHandoffEnvKeys`（恰好 4 个条目、key/value 与 Session 字段一一对应）、`TestEnvForSessionHandlesValuesWithEquals`（值含 `=`（base64 padding `abc==`、query string、nonce `n=ce`）时被完整保留，因消费方只按首个 `=` 切分）。gofmt 干净，restart 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮为自重启握手环境契约补测试安全网，不改变运行时行为。

- 2026-06-12 — Wave 404（vnet protocol 纯 helper focused 覆盖）继续基础设施包审计，发现 `internal/vnet/protocol.go` 的若干纯 helper（`ParseEndpoints`/`JSONByteLength`/`StringValue`/`IntValue`/`Int64Value`）虽被 relay hub 测试间接走到，但**值强制转换与端点解析的边界从未被直接断言**——这些 helper 解码松类型 WS JSON payload 字段（JSON number 到达时是 float64/json.Number），coercion 正确性是协议解析 parity 的关键。新增 `internal/vnet/protocol_helpers_test.go` 3 测试：`TestValueCoercionHelpers`（StringValue trim/非字符串→空、IntValue/Int64Value 对 int/int64/float64/json.Number/非数字的处理）、`TestParseEndpoints`（非数组→nil、只保留 relay/udp transport、跳过非对象项与未知 transport、port/observedAt 从 float64 强制转换）、`TestJSONByteLength`（可序列化值返回 JSON 长度、不可序列化值（channel）返回大值以超出任何限制）。gofmt 干净，vnet 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮为 vnet 协议 helper 补测试安全网，不改变运行时行为。

- 2026-06-12 — Wave 407（slugify Unicode/长度 parity 修复：CJK 保留 + 30 上限）**真实行为修复**——审计发现 Go `internal/chapters/service.go` 的 `slugify`（章节分支/worktree 命名，6 处调用）与 TS `server/lib/slug.ts` 行为不一致：TS NFKD 规范化 + 去变音符 + 保留 Unicode 字母/数字（`\p{L}\p{N}`，CJK 不丢）+ 30 字符上限；Go 旧实现只保留 `[a-z0-9]`（CJK 全丢→纯 CJK 标题退化为 "chapter"）+ 48 上限。例：`"重构 API"` TS 得 `重构-api`、Go 旧得 `api`；纯 CJK `"测试"` TS 得 `测试`、Go 旧得 `chapter`——分支名 parity 真实破裂。修复：用已在依赖中的 `golang.org/x/text`（norm/runes/transform，已确认离线可用）重写 slugify 匹配 TS 语义——NFKD 规范化 → `runes.Remove(runes.In(unicode.Mn))` 去组合变音符 → `[^\p{L}\p{N}]+`→`-` → trim dash → lowercase → 按 rune 截断 30 + 尾 dash trim → 空回退 "chapter"。新增 `internal/chapters/slugify_test.go` 2 测试：`TestSlugifyMatchesTypeScriptSemantics`（ascii/空白/特殊字符压缩/纯符号→chapter/**CJK 保留**（重构-api、测试）/变音符去除（café→cafe）/数字保留）、`TestSlugifyCapsAtThirtyRunes`（长 ascii 与长 CJK 均 ≤30 rune 且无尾 dash）。既有 chapters 测试用硬编码分支名（非 slugify 输出）故不受影响。gofmt 干净，chapters 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。**诚实边界**：唯一保留的细微差别是超长多字节标题的精确截断位——Go 按 rune 截断、TS `.slice(0,30)` 按 UTF-16 code unit，对 BMP 内 CJK/拉丁无差异，仅在含 astral-plane 字符的超长标题上可能差一两位，但结果均为合法确定的分支名。注：`golang.org/x/text` 在 go.mod 仍标 `// indirect`（离线无法 `go mod tidy` 提升为 direct，不影响构建/测试，联网时会自动提升）。
- 2026-06-12 — Wave 408（isInsidePath 路径包含安全 helper focused 覆盖）继续纯逻辑审计，发现两处 `isInsidePath`（安全相关路径包含检查）**直接断言缺失**：`internal/chapters/service.go`（compose 文件解析、copy src/dest 校验、worktree 安全）与 `internal/simplecontent/handlers.go`（skill companion 文件读取边界）——此前仅经 traversal-rejection 路由测试间接走到，包含语义本身未被锁定。两者有一处**有意的语义差异**值得 pin：chapters 版 `rel="."`（base 自身）返回 false（严格要求"内部"而非"自身"），simplecontent 版 `root==target` 返回 true（skill 目录自身是有效 root）。新增 `internal/chapters/is_inside_path_test.go` 2 测试（`TestIsInsidePathContainmentSemantics`：直接/嵌套子项 inside、base 自身 not-inside、parent escape/dotdot/sibling-shared-prefix 均 false；`TestIsInsidePathMustResolvesRelativeInputs`：absolve 后相对路径判断）与 `internal/simplecontent/is_inside_path_test.go` 1 测试（`TestIsInsidePathSkillContainment`：exact-root inside、子项 inside、escape/traversal/sibling 拒绝）。gofmt 干净，chapters/simplecontent 包测试、`GOPROXY=off go build ./...` 与全套件 `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` 零失败，`git diff --check` 干净。本轮为两处安全相关路径包含 helper 补测试安全网并锁定其（有意不同的）same-path 语义，不改变运行时行为。
