Fixed Go route drift: missing apply now returns 404 before Podman fallback, create validates required fields and chapter ownership before fallback, nullable description updates can clear to `null`, and unsupported Podman snapshot mutations now return `501` fallback instead of `200`, preventing frontend success toasts for disabled runtime operations.
- Browser-session empty list coverage is now sampled with the seeded narrator fixture: `GET /api/narrators/:id/browser-sessions` returns the TS-compatible direct array shape when no sessions exist. Fixed Go route/runtime drift: browser-session observer broadcasts lifecycle/count events even when optional Go-owned persistence is disabled, live close/TTL/screenshot/stop-tracing attempts now run before storage fallback, persisted missing close/TTL rows now return 404 instead of `200 {ok:false}`, TTL update response uses TS-style `{ok, session}`, stop/close responses use `{ok:true}`, and screenshots now include `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- Previously observed `POST /api/update/download` SSE mismatch is gone in the latest harness: both TS and Go return `404 application/json {error:"No update available"}` with no SSE events for the fresh dev/contract build no-update case; Go still keeps SSE for actual trusted release downloads.
- WS baseline pass remains: `/ws/narrator` and `/ws/terminal` both open successfully with the TS-issued token after switching to the Go backend on the same DB/settings.
- Current raw diff labels after TS-required coverage comparison: none.

#### Required Diff Harness Shape

Wave 89 exposes this as `/api/health.capabilities.contractHarness`: the harness is sampled JSON/SSE/WS coverage over the same HOME/DB/token, uses key-order-insensitive required-shape and semantic assertions, allows additive Go fields, and explicitly does **not** embed the latest executable result or claim full route inventory, browser frontend E2E, live provider, or real runtime side-effect coverage. - **JSON fixtures:** run the same request sequence against TS and Go with the same seeded DB and compare
  status code, content type, required fields, error code, and side effects. Extra Go fields are allowed
  only when frontend consumers ignore them.
- **SSE fixtures:** compare ordered event names plus terminal-result semantics for project clone,
  update download, storage scan, and Kiro chat. Error paths must emit a parseable terminal `error`
  event instead of abruptly ending the stream.
- **WebSocket fixtures:** compare frontend-consumed message names and required fields, not every

### Acceptance Criteria

- Public/auth boundaries match TS exactly, especially `/api/notification-sounds` mutation routes
  and dependency install admin-only behavior.
- Go implements the TS Git-unavailable guard contract for all TS `GIT_REQUIRED_RULES` routes, with
  the same 503 `GIT_NOT_INSTALLED` response shape and no over-blocking of setup/provider/search
  routes.
- A generated or manually reviewed route inventory maps every TS `server/app.ts` mount and
  `server/routes/*.ts` endpoint to one of: full parity, intentional fallback, additive Go-only,
  or unsupported. Unsupported/fallback entries must be accepted by the relevant owner track.
- For P1 degraded groups, endpoint-level smoke tests verify status code, JSON/SSE content type,
  required fields, and auth requirements against TS-compatible expectations.
- No Go-only route changes are treated as parity unless the corresponding frontend or migration
  plan explicitly adopts them.

  but several are fallback/degraded, especially ruler, routines, MCP/custom subagents, terminal WS,
  narrator WS, and field/event semantics.
- No source files other than this spec were modified.

## Track: DB / Schema / FTS Parity

Owner: spec-db
Status: Done
Last update: 2026-05-14

### Scope

- Compared `server/db/schema.ts`, `server/db/fts.ts`, and migration strategy with `go_backend/internal/db/*` and direct SQL in `go_backend/internal/**`.
- Tracked schema field mismatches, enum mismatches, unsupported tables, FTS/search behavior, and migration/no-migration implications.
- Investigation was read-only: no real database connection, no migration execution, no `drizzle/` edits.

### Evidence Summary

- Canonical TS schema defines 38 main tables in `server/db/schema.ts:4`, `server/db/schema.ts:26`, `server/db/schema.ts:49`, `server/db/schema.ts:133`, `server/db/schema.ts:161`, `server/db/schema.ts:205`, `server/db/schema.ts:307`, `server/db/schema.ts:335`, `server/db/schema.ts:381`, `server/db/schema.ts:407`, `server/db/schema.ts:432`, `server/db/schema.ts:492`, `server/db/schema.ts:520`, `server/db/schema.ts:537`, `server/db/schema.ts:562`, `server/db/schema.ts:584`, `server/db/schema.ts:592`, `server/db/schema.ts:653`, `server/db/schema.ts:668`, `server/db/schema.ts:684`, `server/db/schema.ts:703`, `server/db/schema.ts:727`, `server/db/schema.ts:750`, `server/db/schema.ts:773`, `server/db/schema.ts:796`, `server/db/schema.ts:814`, `server/db/schema.ts:833`, `server/db/schema.ts:859`, `server/db/schema.ts:879`, `server/db/schema.ts:909`, `server/db/schema.ts:926`, `server/db/schema.ts:976`, `server/db/schema.ts:995`, `server/db/schema.ts:1028`, `server/db/schema.ts:1062`, `server/db/schema.ts:1071`, `server/db/schema.ts:1099`, `server/db/schema.ts:1137`.
- Go main DB intentionally does not create or migrate schema: `go_backend/internal/db/schema.go:5` returns nil, and `go_backend/internal/db/db.go:42` states the Go compatibility backend must use the TS-created schema.
- Go SQLite setup still mutates connection/database pragmas (`journal_mode = WAL`, `foreign_keys = ON`, `busy_timeout = 250`) in `go_backend/internal/db/db.go:49`; no table/column migration is present in `go_backend/internal/db/*`.
- TS FTS creates trigram FTS5 virtual tables and triggers for `chapters`, `narrator_messages`, and `narrators` in `server/db/fts.ts:47`, `server/db/fts.ts:52`, `server/db/fts.ts:57`, `server/db/fts.ts:64`, `server/db/fts.ts:86`, `server/db/fts.ts:108`; it also detects non-trigram legacy FTS tables and rebuilds after migration/unclean shutdown in `server/db/fts.ts:34` and `server/db/fts.ts:133`.
- TS search uses sanitized FTS5 `MATCH`, `snippet(...)`, and rank ordering with LIKE fallback for short queries in `server/services/search-service.ts:31`, `server/services/search-service.ts:54`, `server/services/search-service.ts:84`, `server/services/search-service.ts:122`, `server/services/search-service.ts:171`.
- Go search does not use the FTS virtual tables; it uses `LIKE` scans for projects, chapters, messages, and narrators in `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:41`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:73`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:100`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:118`.
- Go project backup/import has an independent project-local schema for a project-domain portability subset in `go_backend/internal/projects/service.go:1154`; it includes project/chapter/edge/narrator transcript/tool/review/terminal/container/volume snapshot tables but is not the main DB migration path and is not a full main-DB export.
- Go direct SQL uses newer TS tables such as `workspaces`, `hooks`, `volume_snapshots`, `volume_snapshot_applications`, `api_requests`, `background_tasks`, `gateway_session_mappings`, and narrator permission/snapshot tables, relying on their presence in the TS-created DB (`go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:123`, `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:228`, `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:2425`, `go_backend/internal/systemroutes/handlers.go:2565`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:2491`, `go_backend/internal/systemroutes/handlers.go:590`).
- Optional/non-canonical stores are now explicit capability entries rather than hidden main-schema assumptions: custom subagents use filesystem `SUBAGENT.md` definitions, MCP server settings use `settings.json`, and browser sessions use a Go-owned optional native-extension runtime table (`go_backend/internal/misccompat/custom_subagents.go`, `go_backend/internal/mcpcompat/handlers.go`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/browser_sessions.go`).

### Findings

- **Main schema ownership is intentionally TS-only.** This is compatible with a “compatibility backend” if users always start from a TS-migrated database, but it is not a standalone replacement: a fresh Go-only install will not create the 38 canonical tables or FTS objects.
- **FTS/search parity is incomplete.** TS relies on FTS5 trigram tables with `MATCH`, `snippet`, and rank-based scoring; Go returns superficially similar search result shapes but falls back to unindexed `LIKE`, fixed scores, no FTS snippets, no rank ordering, and potentially worse CJK/substring behavior.
- **Schema table coverage is mostly by assumption, not by Go guard.** Because Go does not run migrations, direct SQL against canonical tables will fail if the TS schema is older than the Go code expects. Some optional tables have guards (`custom_subagents`, `mcp_servers`), but many TS schema tables used directly by Go do not have version/capability checks.
- **Enum parity is only partially enforced.** SQLite has no CHECK constraints in either Drizzle definitions or Go direct SQL; Go writes several enum-like literals (`review`, `merge`, `fork`, `running`, `cancelled`, etc.) that match observed TS enum values, but invalid values are not prevented at DB level. Tests also contain non-canonical literals such as `permission_mode = 'trust-all'` and `narrator_tool_calls.status = 'approved'` in `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_integration_test.go:458` and `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_integration_test.go:537`; these are test-only evidence of drift risk, not observed production writes.
- **Project-local backup DB is a deliberate subset.** It should not be mistaken for schema parity: it omits messages, tool calls, terminals, user preferences, review conclusions, volume snapshots, hooks, API requests, background tasks, etc.

### P0

- **P0 — Search/FTS behavior is not replacement-equivalent.**
  - Partial: Go `/api/search` now attempts TS-created FTS5, falls back to LIKE with explicit degraded metadata when FTS is unavailable, treats short-query LIKE mode as normal non-degraded behavior, preserves invalid-entity empty-result behavior, and has focused tests for ready FTS metadata/rank/snippets plus CJK trigram substring hits. Wave 44 added first-class health metadata under `search`: `ftsRequiresTypeScriptSetup:true`, `ftsReadinessPerRequest:true`, `ftsRepair:false`, `ftsRebuild:false`, `ftsCleanShutdownMarker:false`, `ftsOldTokenizerRepair:false`, and `fullTSFTSLifecycleParity:false`, so existing-FTS/LIKE behavior is not mistaken for TS FTS lifecycle ownership. Wave 72 adds health-visible query/entity/result/metadata/fallback/ranking contracts so frontend/replacement gates do not infer untested response breadth from the lifecycle flags alone. Wave 88 adds `search.smokeContract` to distinguish the clean minimal TS-Go CJK/short-query fixture from unclaimed larger-realistic relevance, degraded-large-data, missing/corrupt FTS, and full DB-FTS-01 coverage. Wave 95 adds `search.largeDataRelevanceContract` to make the remaining corpus requirements concrete: chapter/narrator/message collisions, CJK trigram substrings, timestamp tie breakers, missing-or-corrupt FTS copies, and explicit false flags for TS-Go endpoint smoke, ranking/order diff, large-data degraded metadata, and no-5xx fallback proof. Wave 120 adds `search.replacementContract`: minimal TS-created CJK/short-query proof, ready FTS metadata/rank/snippet behavior, invalid-entity empty results, and explicit LIKE fallback metadata are sampled; Wave 173 expands that proof to a larger TS-created corpus and flips `largerRealisticRelevanceSmoke:true`, `tsGoEndpointSmoke:true`, `rankingOrderDiffClean:true`, and `multiEntityTopKOrderAsserted:true`; Wave 174 flips `missingCorruptFTSNo5xxSmoke:true` with focused large missing/corrupt FTS fallback fixtures; Wave 175 flips `frontendAdditiveMetadataFullSmoke:true` with frontend Search API/runtime-status smoke. Wave 176 flips `ftsLifecycleExplicitProductDecision:true` and exposes `search.ftsLifecycleProductGate` so the lifecycle decision is closed without claiming Go ownership. Wave 177 flips `crossEngineSearchExplicitProductDecision:true` and exposes `search.crossEngineProductGate` so non-SQLite/Postgres LIKE compatibility is explicit without claiming engine-agnostic SQLite FTS golden parity. Wave 266 closes a later top-k order regression by rounding rank scores with TS-style semantics and using hidden raw FTS rank as a same-type tie-break before timestamp fallback, exposing `search.rankingContract.rawFTSRankTieBreakWithinType:true`. Remaining blockers before Search can be treated as replacement-safe are `ftsLifecycleOwnershipParity:false` and `crossEngineSearchGoldenParity:false` by design, `fullDBFTS01Parity:false`, and `replacementSafe:false`.
  - Evidence: TS FTS setup in `server/db/fts.ts:47`, `server/db/fts.ts:52`, `server/db/fts.ts:57`; TS search in `server/services/search-service.ts:54`, `server/services/search-service.ts:84`, `server/services/search-service.ts:122`; Go search in `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:79-155`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:187-223`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:268-303`, `go_backend/internal/search/handlers.go:340-382`; capability in `go_backend/internal/app/router.go`; tests in `go_backend/internal/app/route_contract_test.go` and `go_backend/internal/search/handlers_contract_test.go:13-219`.
  - Acceptance: Keep Go search on existing TS-created FTS tables with compatible sanitized `MATCH`/snippet/rank semantics and LIKE fallback for <3 chars, keep unsupported FTS setup/rebuild/repair/clean-shutdown ownership explicit in health, and finish DB-FTS-01 by adding replacement acceptance after Wave 173's larger corpus/order endpoint proof, Wave 174's missing/corrupt FTS no-5xx fallback proof, Wave 175's frontend additive metadata tolerance proof, Wave 176's explicit lifecycle product gate, and Wave 177's explicit cross-engine product gate.


- **P0 — No default Go-side schema/bootstrap path for standalone replacement.**
  - Gap: `go_backend/internal/db/schema.go` intentionally does nothing; default SQLite startup is read-only TS-owned compatibility mode and refuses missing/invalid canonical TS databases instead of creating or migrating the business schema. Wave 46 made this machine-readable in health with `sqliteDefaultBootstrapMode:"read-only-ts-owned"`, `sqliteFreshInstallDefaultSupported:false`, `requiresExistingTypeScriptDatabase:true`, `businessSchemaCreationDefault:false`, `businessSchemaMigrationDefault:false`, `startupBackfillRepair:false`, `mainDBRepair:false`, and `defaultFailureMode:"startup-readiness-error"`. Wave 87 adds `database.migrationCompatibilityContract`, `migrationFailureContract`, `repairContract`, `ftsReadinessContract`, and `oldDataBackfillContract`, making the exact startup order, accepted migration journals, Go-journal-only write policy, missing DB/journal/hash/schema/FTS actions, TypeScript repair steps, and non-Go old-data backfill ownership visible at runtime. Wave 121 adds `database.replacementContract`: canonical schema startup guard, migration compatibility/default-strict contracts, failure-mode user actions, TypeScript repair ownership, FTS readiness, and legacy helper non-default scope are sampled. Wave 178 adds `database.compatibilityBetaProductGate` / `database.replacementContract.compatibilityBetaProductDecision:true`, accepting scoped compatibility beta only as `ts-migrated-db-only` with UI/release gates that require an existing TypeScript database and repair path. `standaloneGoFreshInstallSupported:false`, `goBusinessSchemaCreation:false`, `goBusinessSchemaMigration:false`, `goFTSSetupRepair:false`, `goStartupBackfillRepair:false`, `strictMigrationReleaseCIWired:false`, `fullStandaloneGoReplacementParity:false`, and `replacementSafe:false` remain blockers before a Go-only DB/bootstrap replacement claim. ### Acceptance Criteria

- Go backend startup/readiness states one of two modes explicitly:
  - **Compatibility mode:** refuses or warns clearly when the TS-created schema/FTS objects required by enabled routes are missing or too old; never attempts migrations.
  - **Replacement mode:** owns a safe migration/FTS bootstrap path equivalent to TS/Drizzle plus `server/db/fts.ts`.
- `/api/search` parity has focused Go fixtures covering short-query non-degraded LIKE mode, invalid-entity empty result behavior, FTS-ready metadata/rank/snippets, FTS-missing degraded fallback, special-character sanitization, TS-compatible chapter LIKE snippets, CJK trigram substring matches across chapters/messages/narrators, Wave 173 larger TS-created endpoint order/top-k smoke, Wave 266 same-type raw-rank tie-break top-k repair, and Wave 174 missing/corrupt FTS no-5xx degraded fallback fixtures. Wave 88 health exposes the current TS-Go smoke coverage and explicitly marks full DB-FTS-01 parity false. Wave 95 health exposes `search.largeDataRelevanceContract`; after Wave 177, the lifecycle and cross-engine product decisions are explicit (`search.ftsLifecycleProductGate.acceptedAsLifecycleDecision:true`, `search.crossEngineProductGate.acceptedAsCrossEngineDecision:true`) while Go FTS ownership and engine-agnostic SQLite FTS golden parity remain false by design. Remaining `/api/search` parity requires:
  - full DB-FTS-01 replacement acceptance with the SQLite lifecycle and cross-engine product gates accounted for.
- Direct-SQL production writes are checked against TS table/column/enum expectations, with Go constants or validators for every enum-like value written by Go. Wave 90 makes the current representative enum/write audit health-visible, but full acceptance still requires centralized constants/validators and exhaustive production-write proof.
- Project backup/import scope is documented and tested as either a subset or expanded to full TS parity.
- Optional tables not in `server/db/schema.ts` (`custom_subagents`, `mcp_servers`) have explicit ownership and user-facing degradation behavior.

### Notes

- Main DB “no migrations in Go” is not itself a bug if replacement readiness is scoped to a compatibility backend over a TS-migrated database; it becomes P0 only for standalone replacement claims.
- `go_backend/internal/db/db.go:49` sets connection pragmas including `busy_timeout = 250`, which aligns with the project rule to avoid multi-second SQLite busy blocking.
- No real DB was opened for verification; evidence is static code only.

### Go DB Compatibility Contract

Go backend DB handling is intentionally read-only over a TS/Bun-owned main database. Wave 178 makes this a scoped compatibility-beta product gate, not a standalone replacement claim: Go may run only when an existing TS-migrated database and TypeScript repair path are available. The contract below is the operational rule set that makes the compatibility backend safe:

1. **Startup order is fixed.** `go_backend/internal/db/db.go` must open the SQLite file, then run `VerifyCanonicalSchema`, then run `EnsureMigrationCompatibility`. If any step fails, Go must close the DB and refuse to serve requests.
2. **Go never repairs the main schema.** `go_backend/internal/db/schema.go` remains a no-op for main schema creation/migration. Go may only write its own namespaced compatibility journal table `__narrafork_go_migration_journal` after schema readiness has passed.
3. **Canonical schema is the readiness gate.** `CanonicalSchemaContract()` is the read-only contract report. It must continue to describe the TS-owned main schema, required indexes, FKs, FTS tables, and FTS sync triggers that enabled Go routes rely on.
4. **Migration compatibility is journal-based, not migration-based.** `EnsureMigrationCompatibility()` compares the embedded Drizzle snapshot against the existing TS journal `__drizzle_migrations`. It does not execute Drizzle SQL. Missing expected hashes block startup; extra unknown hashes are recorded in the Go journal but do not mutate TS-owned business tables. Wave 87 makes the runtime contract explicit: default startup runs open → pragmas → runtime sidecar → `VerifyCanonicalSchema` → `EnsureMigrationCompatibility`, records only `__narrafork_go_migration_journal` after success, and strict mode rejects extra hashes without writing that journal.
5. **FTS is diagnostic-only in Go.** Go does not rebuild or repair FTS. If FTS tables/triggers are missing or incompatible, the readiness check must fail with an actionable message telling the user to start the TypeScript backend once so TS can run migrations, `ensureColumns`, backfills, and `ensureFts`. Wave 87 distinguishes this startup-fatal FTS readiness from `/api/search`'s request-time LIKE fallback, which is only for startup-bypass/test/runtime-drift scenarios.
6. **Legacy data repair remains TS-owned.** Old narrator status values, missing columns, malformed JSON trait fields, and clean-shutdown/FTS rebuild recovery stay in the TS startup path. Go may detect them and refuse startup, but it must not patch them.

#### DB Compatibility Decision Table

| Condition | Go behavior | User action |
|---|---|---|
| `narrafork.db` missing | fail fast before opening | start TS backend once to create and migrate the DB |
| `__drizzle_migrations` missing | fail fast with migration guidance | run `bun run db:migrate` or start TS backend once |
| Expected Drizzle hash missing | fail fast with the missing tag/hash list | start TS backend once so migrations are applied |
| Extra unknown Drizzle hashes present | allow startup, record them in Go journal | treat as forward-compatible only if canonical schema still passes |
| Canonical table/column/index/FK mismatch | fail fast with read-only schema mismatch details | run TS backend once to migrate/backfill the database |
| FTS table/trigger/tokenizer mismatch | fail fast with FTS readiness diagnostics | run TS backend once so `ensureFts` can rebuild or validate the FTS objects |
| Legacy narrator status/traits/substatus shape | fail fast if readiness is broken | run TS backend once so `ensureColumns` and data backfill can normalize the rows |

#### TS Repair Instruction

When Go reports a DB compatibility failure, the expected recovery path is:

1. Start the TypeScript backend once on the same database, or run `bun run db:migrate` followed by `bun run dev`/`bun run start`.
2. Let TS complete `runMigrations`, `ensureColumns`, the narrator status/trait backfill, and `ensureFts`.
3. Re-run Go startup. If Go still fails, the remaining issue is a real schema drift, not a missing TS repair step.

### Track: Domain Lifecycle Parity

Owner: spec-domain
Status: Done
Last update: 2026-05-14

### Scope

- Compared lifecycle behavior in the requested TS surface:
  `server/services/{project,chapter,chapter-fork,chapter-merge,chapter-batch-merge,review,git,chapter-edge,dependency}*.ts`
  and `server/routes/{projects,chapters,git,graph,ruler,reviews,chapter-edges}.ts`.
- Compared the requested Go surface:
  `go_backend/internal/{projects,chapters,chapteredges,graph,explorations,gitroutes,git}/`.
- Focus: projects, chapters, fork/split/dormant/wake, merge/unmerge/batch merge, review,
  ruler, graph, dependency edges, and git panel lifecycle semantics.

### Verified Findings

- Go has a substantial compatibility backend for basic project/chapter/git flows, but it is not
  lifecycle-parity safe yet. Basic project CRUD, root chapter creation, create/fork/review/merge,
  graph reads, edge CRUD, exploration groups, review conclusion actions, and git
  status/stage/commit/diff/stash/reset exist.
- Highest-risk gaps are semantic lifecycle gaps, not just missing endpoints: project/chapter delete,
  fork-at-message state, review correctness, batch merge orchestration, partial/degraded ruler lifecycle
  semantics, graph update semantics, and git safety differ from the TS backend.

#### Projects lifecycle

- **Implemented in Go:** list/create/get/update/delete plus import/backup endpoints are registered in
  `go_backend/internal/projects/handlers.go:18-27`; create supports `existing`/`init`/`clone`, SSE
  progress for clone, default branch detection, project insert, and root chapter creation in
  `go_backend/internal/projects/service.go:83-137`; backup/import copies a scoped table set in
  `go_backend/internal/projects/service.go:219-341`.
- **TS evidence:** project creation also initializes the per-project backup DB, updates `.gitignore`,
  and commits the backup ignore entry in `server/routes/projects.ts:237-241`; project deletion walks
  every chapter through full cleanup, deletes exploration groups, prunes worktrees, and performs
  fallback FK cleanup in `server/routes/projects.ts:313-443`.
- **Fixed 2026-05-25 / narrowed 2026-06-08 Wave 103:** Go project delete is no longer a direct project-row delete. It collects
  project chapters/narrators/terminals/containers, kills terminal runtimes, removes non-root worktrees,
  prunes worktrees, removes container IDs, deletes broad project-scoped rows (chapters, edges,
  commits, exploration groups, narrators/messages/tool calls/snapshots/buffers, terminals/tabs/view
  state, containers/ports, merge sessions, review conclusions, volume snapshots, hooks, gateway
  mappings, benchmark rows), cleans workspace/recent-tab JSON references, and returns cleanup warning
  details for external failures. Evidence: `go_backend/internal/projects/service.go:312-581`, tests in
  `go_backend/internal/projects/service_delete_test.go:24-104`, and health metadata
  `projects.delete`. Remaining deletion parity gap is external side-effect proof: TS emits async
  recent-tab/proxy-cache refresh flows after delete, while Go now performs DB-side tab cleanup, emits
  `user:recent_tabs_snapshot` for affected users after committed cleanup, refreshes the proxy runtime/cache
  hook for deleted proxy-domain projects, and the frontend invalidates the project list when that snapshot
  removes a project tab. Wave 103 keeps `replacementSafe:false` until live Podman cleanup-warning,
  async refresh parity, and full side-effect parity are available; seeded TS-Go delete DB/FS and real-git worktree warning proofs are now covered.
- **Gap / narrowed 2026-06-08 Wave 109:** Go project create supports existing/init/clone route
  surface, clone SSE `progress`/`credential_required`/`error`/`complete` events, project-row creation,
  best-effort root chapter creation, and root narrator creation. Health exposes this as

#### Fork, split, and cherry-pick lifecycle

- **Implemented in Go:** fork creates branch/worktree, records parent/fork fields, creates a fork edge,

  needs endpoint-level proof against TS fixtures.
- **Implemented in Go, still needs TS-vs-Go fixture proof:** split-at-commit now creates a frozen prefix chapter, preserves the original chapter as the continuation, creates a new fork from the split point, rewires fork edges/child chapters, truncates prefix/new-fork narrator refs at the commit/message cutoff, auto-starts inherited containers only for the new fork, and rolls back DB/git artifacts on transaction failure (`go_backend/internal/chapters/handlers.go:365-400`, `go_backend/internal/chapters/split_at_commit_test.go:21-277`). Remaining split parity risk is endpoint-level TS-vs-Go semantic proof across richer real project histories and true TS AI summary equivalence; the Go compressed fallback is explicit and now includes bounded safe prefix transcript excerpts rather than only a placeholder.

  (`go_backend/internal/chapters/handlers.go:340-372`), but it does not create `cherry_pick` edges or

  commit-row counts, worktree branch/path invariants, review prompt markers, and project graph shape.

### Acceptance Criteria

- **Projects:** create/update/delete/import/backup smoke tests prove DB rows and filesystem side
  effects match TS, including root chapter creation, backup DB setup, `.gitignore`, project deletion
  of all chapter/narrator/terminal/container/merge/exploration resources, and no orphan worktrees.
- **Chapters:** create, update, delete, cleanup, dormant, and wake enforce TS invariants; root/trunk
  cannot be invalidly mutated; status transitions create/remove worktrees and emit/broadcast the same
  observable lifecycle outcomes as TS or are explicitly unsupported.
- **Fork/split:** Go either implements `full`/`compressed`/`fresh`, `forkAtMessageUuid`, file snapshot
  replay, commit copy, copyFiles, containers/startup script, and edge metadata, or rejects unsupported
  modes before mutating git/DB.
- **Merge/batch/review:** merge, AI resolve, unmerge, batch merge, and review pass TS-compatible smoke
  flows including conflict handling, stale review protection, merge summaries/events, interactive
  batch decisions, rollback, review conclusion/promote/dismiss/convert, and no git/DB divergence after
  forced failures.
- **Ruler:** Go ruler compatibility routes are smoke tested against TS lifecycle expectations,
  especially temp-chapter AI conflict resolution and merge/rebase semantics, or their partial/degraded
  behavior is explicit enough that the frontend cannot enter a misleading broken lifecycle. Wave 112
  satisfies the explicit-degraded health contract layer, and Wave 143 rolls it into `ruler.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`; full
  replacement still needs true temp-chapter AI
  and full TS Ruler lifecycle parity; Wave256 covers the focused frontend lifecycle no-crash smoke, Wave257 covers bounded segment/position/project-graph endpoint diff, Wave258 covers merged-branch deletion, Wave259 covers trunk dirty-check parity, Wave260 covers rebase-conflict narrator prompt/locale parity, Wave261 covers explicit AIResolve fallback payloads while temp-chapter parity remains false, Wave262 covers focused Go DB/Git/graph mutation-lifecycle side effects, Wave263 makes the temp-chapter AI fallback contract explicit, and Wave264 covers the shared TS-Go Ruler merge DB/Git/graph diff smoke.
- **Graph/edges/git:** graph returns dependency update state and compatible positions; dependency
  edge validation remains covered by Go parity tests; Wave 152 keeps dependency lifecycle replacement
  unsafe under `chapters.dependencies.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` until
  event/project-DB-sync/status-sync-upstream/frontend-refresh/DB-Git metadata proofs exist;
  git panel operations match route semantics and safety expectations, with exact response-shape/API
  details coordinated with API Contract Parity and Frontend Contract / Smoke Tests.

### Notes

- Evidence is code-read only; no DB, migrations, tests, or runtime commands were executed.
- Several gaps cross tracks: response shapes/status codes belong to API Contract Parity; review/AI
  resolver and fork inherit-mode behavior also overlap Narrator / Agent Parity; event bus/WebSocket,
  terminal, container, proxy-cache, and filesystem side effects overlap Runtime / System Integration
  Parity; UI smoke coverage belongs to Frontend Contract / Smoke Tests.


## Track: Narrator / Agent Parity

Owner: spec-narrator
Status: Done
Last update: 2026-05-14

### Scope

- Compared narrator REST/SSE/WS behavior, message storage, tool call persistence, permission system, plan mode, subagents, hooks, sidecars, compact/recovery, usage/cost, provider runtime, buffers, and goals.
- Investigation scope was limited to `server/routes/narrators.ts`, `server/services/narrator-*.ts`, `server/services/subagent-*.ts`, `server/lib/agent/**`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/*`, `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/*`, and `go_backend/internal/ws/*`.

### Verified Findings

- **Route surface is broad but still compatibility-oriented, not full TS parity.** Go registers many narrator routes including messages, permissions, goals, buffer, custom traits, compact, plan mode, fork, retry, background tasks, browser/session placeholders, and subagent detach (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:32-141`). Several implementations are simplified direct SQL or stubs (`leave` returns `{ok:true}` at `handlers.go:1843-1845`; `notImplemented` helper at `handlers.go:1738-1740`). TS route imports show the original route delegates to many session services for buffered messages, active sessions, compact, permission reprocessing, temporary model restore, plan reflection, and edit/regenerate (`server/routes/narrators.ts:146-177`).
- **Message storage parity is partial.** Go uses `narrator_messages` + `narrator_message_refs`, supports seq cursor pagination, top-level filtering, child messages via `parent_tool_use_id`, and tree building (`go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:451-508`, `624-668`, `671-752`). Go also copies refs during fork (`service.go:196-256`, `363-378`) and hydrates stored sidecars into message/tool/content-block responses. TS has the same core junction-table pattern and message-version bumping (`server/services/narrator-persistence.ts:51-129`, `157-199`) plus richer session/runtime use of catch-up cursors and sidecar-aware message loading (`server/services/narrator-messages.ts:118-222`). Wave 343 makes the focused Go message-history proof health-visible: cursor older/newer paging, `around` windows, child tree/tool-call attachment, child-anchor catch-up cursors, route `orphanChildren`/`messageVersion` metadata, and TS-compatible `messageUuid` aliases. Remaining message-storage risk is broader live-session catch-up breadth, creator enrichment for all paths, and provider/live fixture proof rather than absence of content-block storage/replay.
- **Agent history replay is narrowed but still fixture-gated.** Go no longer builds provider history from `content_text` only: `agentHistory` replays stored `content_json`, reconstructs assistant `tool_use`, user `tool_result`, completed `narrator_tool_calls`, supported reasoning/redacted thinking, compact summary boundaries, sidecar text, trailing tool results, uploaded user images as provider-visible base64 image inputs, `text_file` attachment hints from provider-effective `contentText`, native `web_search` context/Responses items, OpenAI Responses `image_generation_call` replay, and TS-compatible `parentToolUseId` filtering/subagent top-level replay (`go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:2991-3044`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_history_content.go`; `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/history_content.go`; `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_openai_chat.go`; focused tests in `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_history_content_test.go` and `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/history_content_test.go`). The TS-created long-transcript/shared-ref fixture is now health-visible via Wave 339; remaining replay gaps are provider-specific live smoke for rich/native blocks rather than the previous core tool transcript loss.
- **Tool call persistence is partial.** Go records running/pending/completed `narrator_tool_calls`, input/output JSON, file snapshots, and links results to the assistant draft (`go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_handler.go:227-274`; permission records in `service.go:928-966`). Go now blocks malformed/truncated tool input (including `_raw`) and empty `Write`/`Edit` input before execution, records failed tool results, and runs `PreToolUse` hooks before those malformed/empty-input guards so hook payloads can inspect `_raw` like TS. Remaining gaps are provider-specific live-session breadth for progress/long-running output streaming, redirected inputs outside covered plan-mode paths, usage/cost linkage, and full permission suggestion lifecycle. TS executor explicitly performs permission handling, `PreToolUse` hooks, malformed/truncated JSON guards, progress/long-running events, throttled output streaming, and redirected-input tracking (`server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:145-308`).
- **Permission behavior is functional but not equivalent.** Go supports pending tool calls, REST/WS decisions, answers merged into `input_json`, a 5-minute wait, danger reflection, status broadcasts, the TS ask-in-passing read-only permission lock, malformed/empty write-input guards, TS-ordered `PreToolUse` before malformed guards, subagent permission broadcasts targeted at the parent narrator, TS-compatible permission-gated readOnly `Agent` restrictions, TS-compatible permission-rank gating for `Send`, TS-style mode-only gating for `TeamStatus`/`AddGoal`/`Terminal`, TS-style path-aware fallback for read-only path tools and unknown tools, hard-deny protection for `.git` write targets including common Bash destructive/redirection writes, Bash destructive hard-deny for project root / root ancestors / `.worktrees` when `project.git_path` is available, mode-sensitive Bash command whitelist decisions, workdir-aware Bash command path blacklist/whitelist sensitivity, catastrophic Bash hard-deny subset, safe Bash readOnly/acceptEdits mode behavior, TS-compatible WebFetch bypass/dontAsk mode behavior, AskUserQuestion always-ask event bookkeeping, plan-mode Markdown write redirection to the designated plan file, first-attempt plan-mode soft-deny with relaxed-plan promotion after approval, TS-style `ExitPlanMode` user approval before leaving plan mode, and bypass-mode reprocessing for already-pending tool calls (`go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:914-966`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:1504-1533`; `go_backend/internal/ws/hub.go:216-312`). It still does not fully match TS reflection loop control, full shell AST structural-path analysis, the full permission modes/whitelist/blacklist matrix, or all permission hooks imported by TS session routing (`server/routes/narrators.ts:137-177`).
- **Plan mode core safety is implemented but still smoke-gated.** Go now preserves `previous_permission_mode`/`plan_file_id`, toggles plan traits and read-only mode, redirects Markdown `Write`/`Edit` calls to the designated plan file, supports first-attempt soft-deny approval that promotes `relaxed_plan`, requires user approval before `ExitPlanMode` leaves plan mode, persists edited-plan/diff metadata, honors `compactAfter`, cancels pending exit approvals on manual exit, and restarts with an explicit continuation prompt after approval. Remaining plan-mode risk is broader TS-vs-Go live-session smoke and edge-case polish, not the core write-redirection or plan-exit approval contract (`go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_integration_test.go:927-1125`, `go_backend/internal/narrators/service_test.go:103-193`).
- **Subagent parity is now targeted but still not full TS-native runtime parity.** Go implements `Agent`, `Send`, `Await`, `TeamStatus`, foreground/background child message trees, detach-to-background parent unblocking, attach/await parent-blocking semantics, background result injection into parent tool calls, stale running recovery, TS-compatible readOnly `Agent` permission restrictions for general vs explore/plan subagents, TS-compatible `Send` auto-allow gating by caller/target permission rank, and TS-style `TeamStatus` mode gating before team messages are delivered. TS still has richer abort-controller swapping, team inbox/file-change state, custom subagent prompts, conclusion-file permission exceptions, recovery/compact retry, and more detailed broadcasts (`server/services/subagent-runner.ts:27-54`, `61-169`, `177-220`; `server/services/subagent-executor.ts:33-46`, `85-131`, `182-240`). Wave 141 keeps custom-subagent replacement unsafe via `customSubagents.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` until runtime prompt injection, default-model/tool-access browser smoke, frontend settings UI smoke, and TS-Go route/runtime diffs are proven or explicitly deferred.
- **Hook runtime parity is narrowed but not complete.** Go now executes enabled `PreToolUse` command/http hooks during narrator tool execution with fail-open semantics and explicit block support, matching the TS executor's blocking hook gate (`server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:181-204`). The ordering now matches TS as well: permission resolves first, then `PreToolUse` sees truncated/malformed raw input (`tool_input._raw`) before Go's malformed/empty-input safety guard rejects execution. Go also triggers `PostToolUse` command/http hooks after tool completion with TS-shaped payload fields (`tool_output`, `tool_is_error`) and non-blocking/fail-open semantics, matching the TS post-tool hook behavior (`server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:351-367`). Wave 141 exposes this boundary as `hooks.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`; remaining hook gaps are broader live-session smoke coverage, hook telemetry/event-bus parity beyond current WS `hook:executed` telemetry, frontend hooks UI smoke, and TS-Go hook runtime diffs.
- **Sidecar parity is narrowed but still incomplete.** Go now hydrates existing `narrator_sidecars` rows into message responses, tool-call records, `tool_use` content blocks, and tool-call detail responses so frontend sidecar notices can render. Go also formats hydrated sidecars into model-visible `<side_car>` blocks for user-message context and tool-result output, the Go agent event handler can persist/broadcast sidecars supplied by `tool_result` / `sidecars` events, Go runtime injects TODO reminder sidecars into the same active loop's fourth completed non-`TaskCreate` tool result, silent-progress reminders are generated after consecutive tool calls without visible text, relaxed-plan reminders are generated for non-read-only tools during relaxed plan mode, and the generic `after_tools` user-message sidecar channel is available at provider boundary. Buffered message sidecars now consume command text, image blocks, and text-file attachment hints instead of only pure text, and the busy REST buffer path persists multipart text files under `~/.narrafork/buffered-files`, stores recoverable metadata, rematerializes attachments into the worktree on consumption, and avoids duplicate attachment hints. Remaining gaps are full tree-sitter edge-case parity for relaxed-plan Bash command classification, broader subagent-side collector breadth, and live cross-platform smoke beyond focused background Bash process-tree tests (`server/lib/agent/sidecar.ts:1-60`; `server/services/narrator-session.ts:1463-1519`; `server/services/subagent-executor.ts:491-575`).
- **Compact exists but is not TS-equivalent.** Go can create compact/segment compact marker messages, set `is_compact`, update `prune_boundary_message_id`, and inject the most recent compact summary into future history (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:867-899`, `1605-1633`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:1112-1143`, `1165-1183`). TS has locks, background/blocking compact modes, context-overflow recovery, prune boundary handling, custom compact, and segment operations wired through session services (`server/services/narrator-session-state.ts:286-301`; `server/services/narrator-recovery.ts:74-115`; `server/routes/narrators.ts:155-168`). Go compact is acceptable as a compatibility marker plus focused context-overflow compact/retry fallback, not a replacement for full TS recovery.
- **Buffers still differ; goals REST/realtime/runtime parity is narrowed but not complete.** Go exposes buffer CRUD and goals CRUD (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go`). TS buffers are runtime-aware, cap queues, persist text files under `~/.narrafork/buffered-files`, support priority front insertion, and coordinate soft-stops (`server/services/narrator-buffer.ts:17-23`, `60-183`). Go goal REST mutations now align with the TS open-goal lifecycle for create/update/remove/clear/reorder and broadcast `goals_set` for frontend consumers, with focused coverage in `go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers_patches_test.go`; Wave 345 makes this health-visible under `narrator.goals` while keeping full runtime parity false. Go also persists/broadcasts bounded `goal_continuation` turns for active goals, injects them into provider history, and accounts active-goal token/time counters from provider usage snapshots with a `goals_set` broadcast after updates. Remaining goal gaps are creator enrichment, relaxed-plan auto-enable/deeper permission interaction, and TS-vs-Go smoke under longer live narrator runs (`server/services/narrator-goal-service.ts:102-153`, `156-188`, `190-240`).
- **Usage/cost parity is incomplete but runtime request/message accounting is narrower.** Go provider events define usage snapshots, metering, API request events, and broadcasts (`go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_types.go:56-85`, `107-173`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_handler.go`), and route `/usage-stats` loads DB stats (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go`). Go now updates active `narrator_goals.tokens_used` / `time_used_seconds`, emits provider-call API request lifecycle events around real adapter calls, persists `api_requests`, links them to the assistant message, writes assistant message usage metadata for available columns, returns a TS-shaped narrator usage summary including direct subagents by default, serves the global usage-history list/detail/stats/timeseries with frontend fields plus fail-open behavior for missing `api_requests`/usage columns, and honors request-dump settings when provider events include raw dumps. Wave 63 exposes this display-oriented boundary in health as `usageHistory.admin`: admin-only/read-only routes, pagination caps, dynamic schema adaptation, unsupported-filter empty results, raw-dump list/detail policy, stats/timeseries field contracts, and `fullTSBillingParity:false`. Wave 137 adds `usageHistory.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`, making the display-vs-billing boundary explicit: admin display shape, dynamic fail-open behavior, raw-dump policy, stats/timeseries shape, and bounded narrator API-request persistence are sampled, but tool-call usage metadata, provider-adapter raw-dump capture coverage, credential/cost accounting breadth, non-narrator request kinds, live-provider usage-history smoke, frontend usage analytics smoke, and full TS billing-service parity remain required before replacement claims. Note: TS currently increments `narrators.total_cost_usd` with `0` in normal session updates, so Go should not invent a different legacy-total-cost aggregation unless the shared contract changes.
- **Provider runtime gap remains P0/P1 depending on configured models.** Go has provider adapters and routing for Anthropic, OpenAI, Kiro, Codex, NKP/NUG/Cline-style providers and fallback logic (`go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_resolver.go:20-61`, `63-220`), but TS provider runtime has richer provider-specific features in `server/lib/agent/**` including sidecars, request dumps, recovery, pipeline state, image generation, and provider-specific event semantics. Go's own resolver still treats unsupported provider prefixes via fallback/error (`provider_resolver.go:50-60`), so replacement safety depends on explicit supported-provider smoke tests.
- **Rollback/edit-regenerate are now active Go routes, not disabled fallbacks.** Go now truncates `narrator_message_refs`/message blocks, marks affected tool calls stale, optionally rebuilds file state from snapshots, preserves shared-message safety with copy-on-write user edits, broadcasts message updates/deletions, and can optionally rerun the agent after an edit (`go_backend/internal/narrators/rollback_edit.go`; focused coverage in `go_backend/internal/narrators/rollback_edit_test.go`). Remaining work is broader TS-vs-Go fixture comparison across provider/runtime edge cases, not a `FEATURE_DISABLED` fallback.

### P0

- **P0-A: Preserve full content-block/tool transcript history.** Core `content_text`-only replay risk is resolved: Go reconstructs provider history from stored `content_json`, completed tool-call rows, compact summaries, sidecars, uploaded image blocks, `text_file` attachment hints, native `web_search` context/Responses items, OpenAI Responses `image_generation_call` items, TS-compatible `parentToolUseId` filtering/subagent replay, and supported reasoning/redacted blocks (`go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:2991-3044`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_history_content.go`; `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/history_content.go`; `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_history_content_test.go`; `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/history_content_test.go`). Wave 339 makes the TS-created long-transcript/shared-ref/image-owner replay smoke health-visible; remaining P0-A replacement risk is provider-specific rich/native block live smoke before claiming transparent replacement for existing tool-heavy narrators.
- **P0-B: Implement TS-equivalent permission safety for writes/exec.** Go must match TS permission modes, whitelist/blacklist behavior, plan-mode semantics, and denial/error persistence before it can safely execute destructive tools. Malformed/truncated input guards, empty `Write`/`Edit` guards, TS-ordered `PreToolUse` before malformed/empty-input guards, permission-gated readOnly `Agent` restrictions, permission-rank-gated `Send`, mode-gated `TeamStatus`/`AddGoal`/`Terminal`, path-aware fallback for external read paths and unknown tools, `.git` write hard-deny protection including common Bash destructive/redirection writes, Bash structural hard-deny for project root / root ancestors / `.worktrees`, mode-sensitive Bash command whitelist behavior, workdir-aware Bash command path blacklist/whitelist sensitivity, catastrophic Bash hard-deny subset, safe Bash readOnly/acceptEdits mode behavior, TS-compatible WebFetch bypass/dontAsk mode behavior, AskUserQuestion/ExitPlanMode always-ask event bookkeeping, Markdown plan-file redirection, and same-narrator bypass-mode pending-permission reprocessing are now covered, but broader TS hook/permission lifecycle and full shell AST parity remain open (`server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:145-308`).
- **P0-C: Plan mode safety contract.** Go now preserves core `previousPermissionMode`/`planFileId` state, redirects Markdown `Write`/`Edit` calls to the plan file, supports first-attempt soft-deny approval that promotes to relaxed plan, requires user approval before `ExitPlanMode` leaves plan mode, refreshes approved tool input so edited plans are applied, stores approved-plan diff metadata, honors `compactAfter` for plan compact persistence, emits TS-style `continueMode`/`continuePrompt` metadata, cancels pending `ExitPlanMode` approvals on manual plan-mode exit, and aborts/restarts after approved `ExitPlanMode` with an explicit continuation user prompt. Remaining gaps are broader live-session TS-vs-Go smoke coverage and edge-case polish rather than the core approval orchestration.
- **P0-D: Provider/runtime allowlist or parity.** If Go is used as replacement, it must either fully support the configured provider/runtime semantics or reject unsupported prefixes before run start with clear UI fallback. Go now defaults to fail-fast for unsupported/unconfigured Kiro/Codex prefixes instead of silently falling back to a different provider; legacy automatic fallback is preserved only behind explicit `agent.allowGoProviderFallback=true` (`go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_resolver.go:52-63`, `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/openai_test.go`). The narrator POST path is covered by a regression test that persists `status=idle`, `substatus=["error"]`, and zero fallback provider HTTP requests for unsupported Kiro (`go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_integration_test.go`). Remaining replacement risk is broader live provider/runtime smoke and frontend messaging polish for fail-fast errors.

### P1

- **P1 rollup / narrowed 2026-06-08 Wave 114 + Wave 123 + Wave 153:**
  `narrator.advancedObservabilityContract` summarizes the current focused proof set across structured
  message history, subagents, hooks, sidecars, compact fallback, usage-history admin display, WS P0/P1
  events/reconnect, rollback/edit side effects, and browser-session lifecycle counts. Wave 153 adds
  `narrator.advancedObservabilityContract.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` so this rollup row is
  itself first-class replacement-unsafe. Wave 123 adds `narrator.replacementContract` as the broader
  replacement boundary and intentionally keeps `replacementSafe:false`. Wave 338 adds a bounded executable provider fixture proof across OpenAI Chat/Responses, Anthropic, Kiro, NKP, NUG, Codex, and Cline, Wave 339 claims the TS-created long-transcript/shared-ref replay fixture, Wave 340 claims bounded foreground tool progress runtime/WS frame proof, Wave 341 claims bounded usage/cost runtime persistence proof, and Wave 342 claims focused context-overflow compact/retry proof, but full replacement still needs provider-specific rich-block/live narrator sessions, full session middleware, full subagent reblocking/abort/parent-promise parity,
  broader compact background/model-summary/recovery parity, full WS provider-specific live smoke, provider-specific foreground tool-progress live breadth,
  rollback/edit long-chain recovery (`narrator.rollbackEditRegenerate.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`), browser live Chrome parity, broader usage/cost credential/toolcall breadth,
  and provider-specific active-run reconnect live-session breadth remain unproven; focused WS reconnect ownership, including version-race second catch_up, is proven under `narrator.wsEvents.unclaimedContract`.
- **P1-A: Subagent execution parity.** Go now covers detach/attach parent-blocking, background task persistence/notifications, result injection, Send/Await continuation, TeamStatus list/file_changes/broadcast/send diagnostics, Await agent/bash supported types, update-conclusion manual-override fallback, and stale recovery. Wave 68 exposes those boundaries under `narrator.subagents` and keeps `fullTSSubagentRuntimeParity:false`; Wave 145 rolls them into `narrator.subagents.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`, so supported routes/tools are not overclaimed as full TypeScript in-memory waiter or parent-promise reblocking parity. Remaining TS parity gaps are abort-controller source swapping, parent-promise reblocking after reattach, custom subagent prompt/default-model runtime, conclusion-file permission exception, richer subagent buffers, broader team inbox/file-change semantics, and compact/recovery polish (`server/services/subagent-runner.ts:27-54`, `61-169`; `server/services/subagent-executor.ts:85-131`, `182-240`).
- **P1-B: Hooks and sidecars.** `PreToolUse` command/http hook execution/blocking semantics, TS-ordered hook execution before malformed/empty-input guards, and `PostToolUse` command/http non-blocking/fail-open payload delivery are now implemented for narrator tools, and Go emits TS-shaped `hook:executed` telemetry after hook execution. Stored/event-supplied sidecars are persisted, broadcast, hydrated into message/tool/detail responses, and injected into model-visible user/tool history. TODO reminder sidecars now also enter the same active loop on the fourth completed non-`TaskCreate` tool result, silent-progress reminders are generated after consecutive tool calls without visible text, relaxed-plan reminders are generated for non-read-only tools during relaxed plan mode, buffered message sidecars now carry command text plus image/text-file attachment metadata, and the generic `after_tools` user-message sidecar channel is wired through provider history. Remaining gaps are event-bus vs WS transport exactness if a future Go event bus is added, full tree-sitter edge-case parity for relaxed-plan Bash command classification, broader subagent collector breadth, and live OS smoke beyond focused background Bash process-tree tests (`server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:181-204`, `server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:351-367`; `server/lib/agent/sidecar.ts:1-60`; `server/services/narrator-session.ts:1463-1519`).
- **P1-C: Compact/recovery parity.** Wave 67 exposes the current compact boundary through `narrator.compact`: route support, marker-message mutations, segment-hidden refs, context/conversation/prune clearing, transactional summary updates, deterministic fallback response fields, compact WS events, disabled model-generated summaries, and `fullTSCompactRecoveryParity:false`. Wave 146 rolls these into `narrator.compact.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`; Wave 342 exposes the focused context-overflow compact/retry smoke without flipping full parity. Remaining work is still TS-equivalent compact locks, background vs blocking compact behavior, broader context-overflow/retry-after-compact breadth, exact prune-boundary semantics, model-generated summary parity, and TS-Go compact recovery diffs; current Go compact remains a marker/summary implementation (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go`).
- **P1-D: Buffer runtime semantics.** Go now covers TS-style max queue limits, priority front insertion, busy-run slash-command command text, persisted multipart text-file attachments with worktree rematerialization, exact-ID reorder validation, zero-based reorder sequence persistence, `buffer_set` broadcasts after update/remove/clear/reorder, and priority-only after-tools cut-in (non-priority buffers now wait for run completion). Wave 65 exposes this route boundary through `narrator.messages`: busy-buffer `202` shape, structured fallback diagnostics, accepted busy statuses, active-run detection, priority after-tools `buffered_user` soft-stop, and `fullTSMessageSendParity:false`; Wave 144 rolls these into `narrator.messages.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`; Wave 345 adds a dedicated `narrator.bufferQueue` focused update contract for update/remove/clear/reorder `buffer_set` broadcasts plus multipart text-file rematerialization while keeping live-session parity false; Wave 346 adds the immediate (non-busy) multipart submit proof and flips `multipartMessageSubmitBreadth:true`; remaining buffer runtime risk is full active-run soft-stop equivalence, provider-specific live-session send smoke, TS-Go DB/WS diffs, and multi-device/WS smoke behavior (`server/services/narrator-buffer.ts:17-183`).
- **P1-E: Usage/cost accounting.** Active-goal token/time counters, provider-call `api_requests`, assistant message usage metadata, narrator `/usage-stats`, global usage-history list/detail/stats/timeseries response shapes, and request-dump persistence policy now expose TS-compatible summary/record fields for frontend usage surfaces and degrade safely on missing usage columns. Wave 63 makes the admin display contract health-visible through `usageHistory.admin` (route support, pagination limits, dynamic schema/fail-open behavior, raw-dump policy, stats/timeseries fields, and `fullTSBillingParity:false`). Wave 137 adds `usageHistory.replacementContract` so those admin/read-only and bounded API-request proofs cannot be treated as full TS billing analytics replacement. Wave 341 also exposes the runtime persistence subset through narrator advanced/replacement health (`usageCostRuntimePersistenceContract:true`) without flipping `fullTSBillingParity:false`. Remaining usage parity is credential metadata/cost breadth, tool-call usage metadata, provider-adapter raw-dump capture breadth, non-narrator request kinds, live-provider/admin usage-history smoke breadth, and frontend usage analytics browser smoke beyond the narrator request/message counters.
- **P1-F: Message catch-up and WS event parity.** Go now has focused WS coverage for subscribe catch-up, `streaming_snapshot`, reconnect queue-state replay for `queue_status` / `nkp_queue_status`, `sync_check`/`sync_ok`/`full_reload`, presence, permission resolution, legacy tool event conversion, preservation of TS optional tool fields (`streamStartedAt`, `parentToolUseId`, `updatedInput`, `metadata`, `sideCars`) in frontend-compatible events, REST goal mutations broadcasting `goals_set`, and bounded `goal_continuation` message/event/runtime turns. Wave 70 locks the health/matrix P0/P1 event-list contract for core message/tool/permission/status/catch-up/sync/streaming-snapshot events plus background task, todos/goals/context/git/tool inventory, metering/quota/queue follow-ups. Wave 93 adds `narrator.wsEvents.unclaimedContract`; Waves 330-336 later claim the focused message mutation, reflection, compact lifecycle, subagent/browser/container status/count, foreground tool runtime, and active-run reconnect frame families while keeping `fullTSWSEventParity:false`; Wave 340 exposes the bounded foreground tool progress runtime/WS frame proof in the broader narrator replacement contract; Wave 343 exposes the REST message-history pagination/catch-up child-anchor contract for cursor older/newer, `around`, orphanChildren, messageVersion, and messageUuid alias metadata while keeping full live catch-up parity false. Wave 344 exposes the focused permission approve/deny updated-input/frame contract while keeping full permission runtime parity false. Remaining parity gaps are broader provider-specific/live-session WS smoke, foreground tool progress under real provider sessions, provider-specific active-run reconnect live-session breadth, broader live catch-up/creator-enrichment breadth, broader live permission/multi-device approval breadth, and longer active-goal continuation/accounting flows.
- **P1-G: Rollback/edit-regenerate fixture breadth.** Focused Go tests now cover refs/block truncation, file-state rebuild and skip-revert paths, rollback-file failure atomicity, copy-on-write edits, image-block preservation, child subagent detachment, compact-boundary preservation, and optional agent rerun. Wave 69 exposes the route/request/preview/response/side-effect/WS contract under `narrator.rollbackEditRegenerate` and keeps `fullTSRollbackEditParity:false`; Wave 147 rolls it into `narrator.rollbackEditRegenerate.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`; add TS-vs-Go fixture diffs for longer tool chains, provider recovery/rerun, active-run edit recovery, frontend rollback/edit smoke, and failure compensation before claiming full replacement parity.

### P2

- **P2-A: Browser/ask-in-passing/custom traits polish.** Go exposes related route names, but parity should be smoke-tested or explicitly scoped as compatibility-only.
- **P2-B: Commands/skills/tool menu fidelity.** Go returns a simplified command/tool menu and reads global skills (`go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:1983-2115`); align with TS slash command resolution and project skill behavior when replacement moves beyond compatibility beta. Wave 140 keeps project routine artifacts and project skill companion output under explicit replacement-unsafe contracts until companion breadth/golden parity and artifact materialization decisions are proven.
- **P2-C: Title generation, retry recovery polish, and fork compressed context quality.** Go implements some route shapes, but should be tested against TS response shapes and user-facing flows.
- **P2-D: Provider-specific nice-to-haves.** Request dumps, image-generation artifacts, queue/quota UX, and model-switch metadata should be matched after P0/P1 safety issues are resolved.

### Evidence

- TS narrator route imports many session-level capabilities: `server/routes/narrators.ts:146-177`.
- TS message persistence/junction refs: `server/services/narrator-persistence.ts:51-129`, `157-199`.
- TS message tree/catch-up support: `server/services/narrator-messages.ts:118-222`.
- TS permission/hook/streaming executor: `server/lib/agent/tool-executor.ts:145-308`.
- TS sidecars: `server/lib/agent/sidecar.ts:1-60`.
- TS plan mode: `server/services/narrator-plan-mode.ts:28-147`.
- TS buffers/goals/subagents: `server/services/narrator-buffer.ts:17-183`, `server/services/narrator-goal-service.ts:102-240`, `server/services/subagent-runner.ts:27-220`, `server/services/subagent-executor.ts:85-240`.
- Go route surface: `go_backend/internal/narrators/handlers.go:32-141`.
- Go message/fork/history: `go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:196-256`, `451-508`, `624-752`, `1105-1162`.
- Go agent run/tool/permission/subagent paths: `go_backend/internal/narrators/service.go:888-1022`, `1360-1738`.
- Go tool-call/event persistence: `go_backend/internal/narrators/agent_event_handler.go:27-99`, `227-274`.
- Go WS permission and broadcast hub: `go_backend/internal/ws/hub.go:488-600`, `1229-1818`; focused WS contract tests in `go_backend/internal/ws/hub_narrator_events_test.go` and `go_backend/internal/ws/frontend_contract_smoke_test.go`.
- Go provider/runtime types and resolver: `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_types.go:56-85`, `107-173`; `go_backend/internal/agentbasic/provider_resolver.go:20-61`, `63-220`.

### Acceptance Criteria

- **Replacement candidate:** all P0 items pass automated or scripted smoke tests using an existing TS-created narrator with tool calls, compact marker, plan mode, pending permission, buffered message, goal, and a subagent tree; no data loss and no unsafe tool execution.
- **Compatibility beta:** if P0-D provider parity is not complete, Go must expose an explicit supported-provider allowlist and fail unsupported narrator models before starting a run, with frontend-visible fallback messaging.
- **Message acceptance:** `GET /api/narrators/:id/messages` and agent replay preserve `content_json` block semantics, nested subagent messages, tool-call IDs, message refs/seq, compact boundaries, and `messageVersion` increments across delete/retry/fork.
- **Permission acceptance:** write/exec tools in default, read-only, plan, and deny modes produce the same pending/approved/denied/cancelled DB rows and WS events as TS; denied or malformed inputs cannot mutate files.
- **Subagent acceptance:** foreground, background, detached, interrupted, messaged, awaited, team-status, file-change, and conclusion-file flows match TS-visible statuses/events and persist background task results.
- **Usage acceptance:** streamed provider usage updates persist to narrator total cost/usage history and per-goal token/time counters, and `/usage-stats` reflects the latest run without requiring TS backend.

### Notes

- Current Go implementation is a useful compatibility backend for simple chat/tool sessions, and core content-block/tool replay plus core plan-mode/provider fail-fast safety are now implemented, but it remains **unsafe as a transparent replacement** for TS in projects relying on provider-specific rich blocks, broader permission/hook lifecycle parity, subagent edge cases, compact/recovery, or runtime side effects without fixture smoke evidence.
- Suggested execution order: P0-B permission/hook lifecycle + plan-mode live smoke → P0-A provider-specific/long-session transcript fixture smoke → P0-D live provider runtime smoke → P1 subagents/hooks/compact/usage → P2 UI polish.

## Track: Runtime / System Integration Parity

Owner: spec-runtime
Status: Done
Last update: 2026-05-14

### Scope

- Compared TS and Go runtime/system surfaces for WebSocket protocols, terminal runtime,
  containers/podman/proxy/ports, uploads/shares/storage/fs, settings/admin/update/runtime,
  providers, MCP, hooks, notifications, gateway, VNet, benchmarks, and volume snapshots.
- Evidence was limited to repository source paths; no server, database, Podman, migration, or
  network runtime was started.

### Findings

- **WebSocket route coverage is still partial, but VNet and terminal reconnect scaffolding are no longer missing.** Go exposes
  `/ws/narrator`, `/ws/terminal`, and `/ws/vnet`; the VNet route is a Go relay hub with JWT/relay-token
  auth, disabled-setting fallback, peer cleanup, peer/message limits, non-upgrade stats, and join/leave
  events, while UDP rendezvous/dataplane remains explicitly unsupported. Wave 54 exposes this as
  `vnet.relayContract` so route presence cannot be misread as TS UDP/runtime parity. Wave 85 further
  exposes `/api/vnet/status` auth/status fields, `/ws/vnet` auth modes, disabled-upgrade drift from TS,
  client/server message types, 1 MiB Go websocket read-limit behavior, relay error codes, and the fact
  that UDP settings/status fields are not a Go UDP rendezvous runtime. Wave 129 adds
  `vnet.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false` so these relay proofs cannot be treated as full VNet replacement readiness. Go terminal WS now covers subscribe/input/resize/kill/create/rename plus
  raw-capped snapshot/scrollback/bufferState replay, snapshot fallback payloads, last-input-client resize
  gating, and backpressure tests. Remaining WebSocket replacement risk is exact TS event/replay fidelity,
  not absent frontend-consumed terminal events. Evidence: `server/websocket/ws-handler.ts:37`,
  `server/websocket/ws-handler.ts:57`, `server/websocket/ws-handler.ts:81`,
  `server/websocket/terminal-ws.ts:78`, `server/websocket/terminal-ws.ts:106`,
  `server/websocket/terminal-ws.ts:159`, `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:192`,
  `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:194`, `go_backend/internal/ws/hub.go:217`,
  `go_backend/internal/ws/hub_terminal_test.go:100`,
  `go_backend/internal/ws/hub_terminal_test.go:189`,
  `go_backend/internal/ws/vnet_relay_test.go:12`, `go_backend/internal/ws/vnet_relay_test.go:59`.
- **Terminal runtime is functional but not TS-equivalent.** TS uses dtach when available, persistent
  `BufferManager` scrollback, platform-specific Bun/portable PTY runtimes, event-bus lifecycle
  events, reattach after detach/restart, and graph-state/admin helpers. Go uses `creack/pty` with an
  in-memory capped raw replay buffer, emits output/exit/requestResize/scrollback/bufferState, updates DB
  status, and exposes capability metadata for raw replay and last-input-client resize. Focused coverage
  now proves snapshots are capped to the newest raw bytes and do not expose serialized xterm state keys.
  Wave 71 also makes the terminal WS contract health-visible via client message, server event,
  subscribe-replay, snapshot fallback, and resize-scope fields, so frontend gates can distinguish
  supported raw replay events from TS serialized replay. Wave 53 makes the replay/dtach boundary nested
  in health through `terminal.replayContract` and `terminal.dtachFallback`, including
  `tsXtermHeadlessParity:false`,
  `xtermSerializedStateKeys:false`, direct PTY fallback, live-socket reattach requirements, and
  startup stale-running cleanup when dtach is unavailable. It still does not provide TS-equivalent
  persistent BufferManager/xterm serialized replay across process restarts, full dtach detach/reattach
  parity on every platform, or TS lifecycle events. Evidence:
  `server/services/terminal-service.ts:171`, `server/services/terminal-service.ts:272`,
  `server/services/terminal-service.ts:277`, `server/services/terminal-service.ts:301`,
  `server/services/terminal-service.ts:337`, `server/services/terminal-service.ts:504`,
  `go_backend/internal/terminalruntime/manager.go:38`,
  `go_backend/internal/terminalruntime/manager.go:76`,
  `go_backend/internal/terminalruntime/manager.go:147`,
  `go_backend/internal/terminalruntime/manager.go:163`.
- **Container routes exist, but orchestration/proxy/port parity still needs end-to-end proof.** TS configures
  rootless Podman environment/cgroup fallback, allocates ports, derives proxy labels/URLs, runs an
  in-process container proxy cache, and emits lifecycle events. Current Go has narrowed the older proxy
  gap: compose-backed live listing derives `proxyLabel`, `proxyUrl`, `containerIp`, and proxy-mode
  `containerPort`, persists them, maintains `port_allocations`, and starts a Go reverse-proxy HTTP/WS
  server when container proxy settings are enabled. Wave 84 makes the route/fallback boundary explicit in
  health: container routes are authenticated and not Git-guarded, `podman/install` is admin-only,
  `logs` has `tail` default/max and unsafe-service-name guards, list fallbacks return direct arrays plus
  `X-Narrafork-Fallback*` headers, action/log fallbacks expose Podman/compose codes, start returns
  immediately and runs a background job, commands are output/timeout bounded, proxy mode requires
  pasta/passt and skips host-port allocation, and Go emits `container_status_changed` plus legacy
  `container:*` frames while keeping full TS event/runtime parity false. Remaining risk is TS-vs-Go
  parity proof for rootless setup/cgroup behavior, proxy cache reconciliation, port allocation/release
  edge cases, and lifecycle events rather than a complete absence of proxy metadata/runtime. Evidence:
  `server/services/container-service.ts:45`, `server/services/container-service.ts:70`,
  `server/services/container-service.ts:15`, `server/services/container-proxy.ts:38`,
  `go_backend/internal/chapters/handlers.go:1072`,
  `go_backend/internal/chapters/handlers.go:1107`,
  `go_backend/internal/chapters/handlers.go:1165`,
  `go_backend/internal/containerproxy/manager.go:100`,
  `go_backend/internal/containerproxy/manager_test.go:47`,
  `go_backend/internal/chapters/container_runtime_test.go:151`.
- **Uploads/shares/storage/fs are compatibility implementations with different guarantees.** Wave 83
  makes the upload/share part of this boundary explicit in `/api/health`: authenticated upload routes
  expose narrator-image/avatar route patterns, Git-guard absence, path traversal and symlink-escape
  guards, regular-file serving, imageId-prefix lookup, and the known cache-header drift from TS
  immutable uploads. Wave 135 exposes `uploads.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`, so guarded
  narrator-image/avatar serving and missing-state baselines cannot imply immutable-cache, browser-cache,
  full upload lifecycle, shared-upload-owner, or frontend reconnect parity. TS shares are ephemeral in memory and cleaned on restart. Current Go share handling
  is no longer a raw unknown-directory fallback: public GET/preview remain unauthenticated, POST share
  creation is mounted behind auth as a Go compatibility wrapper, safe share IDs are enforced, same-root
  bounded metadata can hydrate in-memory records, expired metadata removes disk content, startup calls
  `CleanupStaleShares`, and focused tests cover expired/stale/path-traversal share behavior. Health also
  records the Go-vs-TS differences: TS has no create route, TS defaults to a 500 MiB max share size while
  Go defaults to 4096 MiB, Go previews HTML as escaped preformatted content instead of using the TS
  sanitize-html path, and Go expiry is lazy/startup-cleanup based rather than TS timer parity. Seeded
  upload serving and public share missing-state baselines are also covered by the TS-Go harness. This is
  still a Go-only disk-metadata bridge rather than TS-equivalent process-memory shares or cross-restart
  persistence; Wave 131 exposes `shares.replacementContract.replacementSafe:false`, so replacement needs an explicit product decision plus smoke evidence for restart/expiry/
  privacy behavior, tool-created share consumers, storage-cleanup public routes, and escaped HTML preview. Go storage scan/cleanup is present and conservative for worktrees/containers; TS has
  admin-gated storage cleanup/scan services. Evidence: `server/routes/uploads.ts:1`,
  `server/lib/uploads.ts:58`, `server/routes/shares.ts:1`, `server/lib/shares.ts:18`,
  `server/lib/agent/tools/share-file.ts:131`, `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:85`,
  `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:2565`, `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:208`,
  `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:888`,
  `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:1120`,
  `go_backend/internal/misccompat/handlers.go:1268`,
  `go_backend/internal/misccompat/share_security_test.go:431`,
  `go_backend/internal/misccompat/uploads_security_test.go:1`.
- **Settings/admin/update/runtime/providers are broad but partly fallback/degraded.** Go registers
  settings/admin/update/runtime/provider routes and many provider compatibility endpoints, but update
  self-apply is conditional on restart-handoff/runtime prerequisites and otherwise returns explicit
  manual-only fallback metadata; version metadata is Go-specific, and some provider/model
  behavior depends on cached settings rather than TS live runtime behavior. Evidence:
  `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:129`, `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:130`,
  `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:131`, `go_backend/internal/app/router.go:137`,

  `go_backend/internal/systemroutes/handlers.go:654`,

- **MCP, gateway, VNet relay mode, and benchmarks are not full TS runtimes; volume snapshots still need positive Podman smoke evidence.** TS MCP uses
  `mcpManager` to connect/disconnect/test external servers and list live external tools; Go exposes
  settings-backed server storage, stdio per-call JSON-RPC tool discovery/injection, bounded runtime limits,
  permission mapping, and non-stdio fallback metadata. Wave 136 records this under

  fallback boundaries exposed, and Go disabled-WS/raw-frame behavior not claimed TS-equivalent. Go benchmark
